Java I/O — Streams, Files, RandomAccessFile & Serialization

Lectures 23.1 – 23.9
Streams — the Basic Idea

I/O = Input / Output. A stream is a flow of data between a source and a destination (keyboard, file, network, memory, another thread). Analogy: water flows from a tap (source) through a pipe (stream) into a bucket (destination) — nobody bathes directly under a tap; the bucket = buffer.

  • Data flows as byteseven a roll number 126 is not sent as one number but as separate digits/bytes ("1", "2", "6"), one after another — or as characters in text streams
  • Input streamreads data *into* your program (source → program)
  • Output streamwrites data *out of* your program (program → destination)
  • Buffera memory area (temporary array) connecting source & destination that work at different speeds — buffered streams read/write in big chunks
  • Two familiesByte streams (InputStream/OutputStream, 8-bit, any data: images, audio, objects) and Character streams (Reader/Writer, 16-bit Unicode text)
  • Standard streams (System class)System.in (InputStream – keyboard), System.out (PrintStream – screen), System.err (PrintStream – errors)
Byte streamsCharacter streams
Base classesInputStream / OutputStreamReader / Writer
Unitbyte (0–255)char (Unicode)
File classesFileInputStream / FileOutputStreamFileReader / FileWriter
Use forbinary files, images, serializationtext files
Packageimport java.io.*;         // for learning; in real code import only the classes you use
InputStream & OutputStream — Base Classes
InputStream methodPurpose
int read()next byte (0–255) or -1 at end of stream
int read(byte[] b)fills the array; returns number of bytes read, or -1
int read(byte[] b, int off, int len)partial fill
byte[] readAllBytes()reads everything to the end
byte[] readNBytes(n)up to n bytes (stricter about reading exactly n)
long skip(n)skips n bytes
int available()bytes that can be read without blocking
boolean markSupported()mark/reset supported? (only buffered streams)
mark(limit) / reset()remember a position, return to it later
void close()releases the resource — important
OutputStream methodPurpose
write(int b)one byte
write(byte[] b) / write(b, off, len)many bytes
flush()force buffered data out to the destination
close()flush + release
  • Why mark needs a buffera stream is consumed — a byte you have read is gone. Only a buffered stream keeps extra data in memory so you can mark() and reset()
  • Always closestreams must be closed (or use try-with-resources)
  • Exceptionsall these methods throw the checked IOException → catch or declare throws
java.io Class Hierarchy — How to Remember
InputStream (bytes in)                           OutputStream (bytes out)
 ├─ FileInputStream                               ├─ FileOutputStream
 ├─ ByteArrayInputStream                          ├─ ByteArrayOutputStream
 ├─ PipedInputStream                              ├─ PipedOutputStream
 ├─ SequenceInputStream  (joins streams)          └─ FilterOutputStream
 ├─ ObjectInputStream    (objects)                     ├─ BufferedOutputStream
 └─ FilterInputStream                                  ├─ DataOutputStream
      ├─ BufferedInputStream                           └─ PrintStream
      ├─ DataInputStream
      └─ PushbackInputStream                     ObjectOutputStream

Reader (chars in)                                Writer (chars out)
 ├─ InputStreamReader ── FileReader               ├─ OutputStreamWriter ── FileWriter
 ├─ BufferedReader                                ├─ BufferedWriter
 ├─ CharArrayReader, StringReader                 ├─ CharArrayWriter, StringWriter
 └─ PipedReader                                   └─ PrintWriter, PipedWriter
ClassPurpose
BufferedInputStream/Reader/…adds a buffer – fewer disk hits, faster; BufferedReader.readLine()
PushbackInputStreamread a byte then push it back to read again
DataInputStream/DataOutputStreamread/write primitive types in their own format (readInt, writeFloat, writeUTF)
ObjectInputStream/ObjectOutputStreamread/write whole objects (serialization)
PrintStream / PrintWriterconvenient print/println/printf
PipedInputStream/PipedOutputStreamcommunication between two threads
SequenceInputStreamconcatenate several input streams
ByteArrayInput/OutputStreamstream over a byte array in memory
InputStreamReader / OutputStreamWriterbridge: bytes ↔ characters (with encoding)
File, RandomAccessFilefile info · read/write anywhere in a file
Pattern: every input class has a matching output class. Remember a few and the rest follow. Wrapping = decorator pattern: new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(System.in)).
FileInputStream & FileOutputStream — Read, Write & Copy
Write bytes to a filetry (FileOutputStream fos = new FileOutputStream("data.txt")) {     // overwrites; use (name, true) to APPEND
    fos.write(65);                              // 'A'
    fos.write("Hello Java".getBytes());         // String → bytes
} catch (IOException e) { e.printStackTrace(); }
Read byte by byte / with arraytry (FileInputStream fis = new FileInputStream("data.txt")) {       // FileNotFoundException if missing
    int b;
    while ((b = fis.read()) != -1)              // -1 = end of file
        System.out.print((char) b);

    // faster: read in blocks
    byte[] buf = new byte[1024];
    // int n; while ((n = fis.read(buf)) != -1) System.out.write(buf, 0, n);
} catch (IOException e) { e.printStackTrace(); }        // IOException is CHECKED
Copy any file (image, pdf…)try (FileInputStream in = new FileInputStream("a.jpg");
     FileOutputStream out = new FileOutputStream("copy.jpg")) {
    byte[] buf = new byte[4096];
    int n;
    while ((n = in.read(buf)) != -1) out.write(buf, 0, n);
}
  • Text with charactersFileReader/FileWriter work exactly the same way but on characters
  • try-with-resourcesstreams are used only inside the try block and closed automatically
  • Compile error*unreported exception IOException; must be caught or declared to be thrown*
  • Never forgetFileOutputStream("x") erases an existing file unless you pass true
Challenge A — copy with case changed: read one file and write to another, swapping the case of letters only (ASCII 65–90 = A–Z → +32; 97–122 = a–z → −32). Spaces, digits and new lines stay unchanged.
int b;
while ((b = in.read()) != -1) {
    if (b >= 65 && b <= 90)       out.write(b + 32);     // upper → lower
    else if (b >= 97 && b <= 122) out.write(b - 32);     // lower → upper
    else                          out.write(b);          // unchanged
}
Challenge B — merge two files: source1.txt + source2.txt → merged.txt (source2 appended after source1).
try (FileOutputStream out = new FileOutputStream("merged.txt");
     SequenceInputStream in = new SequenceInputStream(new FileInputStream("source1.txt"),
                                                       new FileInputStream("source2.txt"))) {
    int b; while ((b = in.read()) != -1) out.write(b);
}
Piped Streams — Communication Between Threads

PipedOutputStream (writer) is connected to PipedInputStream (reader): what one thread writes, the other thread reads — a pipe between threads (producer–consumer without wait/notify code).

class Producer extends Thread {
    PipedOutputStream out;  Producer(PipedOutputStream o) { out = o; }
    public void run() {
        try { for (int i = 1; i <= 5; i++) { out.write(i); Thread.sleep(300); } out.close(); }
        catch (Exception e) { }
    }
}
class Consumer extends Thread {
    PipedInputStream in;  Consumer(PipedInputStream i) { in = i; }
    public void run() {
        try { int v; while ((v = in.read()) != -1) System.out.println("Got " + v); }
        catch (IOException e) { }
    }
}
public class PipeDemo {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws IOException {
        PipedOutputStream pos = new PipedOutputStream();
        PipedInputStream  pis = new PipedInputStream(pos);         // connect the two ends
        // or:  pis.connect(pos);
        new Producer(pos).start();
        new Consumer(pis).start();
    }
}
  • Ruleuse the two ends in different threads (in one thread you may deadlock)
  • Character versionPipedWriter / PipedReader
  • HandleIOException (throw from main to keep code neat in demos)
RandomAccessFile — Read/Write at Any Position

Sequential access = read from the first byte onwards, only forward (normal streams; a stream is only for reading *or* writing). Random (direct) access = jump to any byte and read *and/or* write there.

MethodPurpose
new RandomAccessFile("data.txt", "r")mode "r" read-only · "rw" read + write (created if missing)
seek(long pos)move the file pointer to byte pos (0-based)
getFilePointer()current position
length()file size in bytes
skipBytes(n)skip forward n bytes
read() / readByte() / readInt() / readLine() / readUTF()reading (like DataInputStream)
write(int) / writeInt() / writeBytes(String) / writeUTF()writing
setLength(n)truncate/extend
close()
data.txt contains: ABCDEFGHtry (RandomAccessFile raf = new RandomAccessFile("data.txt", "rw")) {
    System.out.println((char) raf.read());       // A  (pointer now at 1)
    raf.skipBytes(3);                            // skip B C D
    System.out.println((char) raf.read());       // E  (pointer at 5)
    raf.seek(2);                                 // go directly to 3rd byte
    System.out.println((char) raf.read());       // C
    raf.seek(raf.length());                      // go to END
    raf.writeBytes("XYZ");                       // append
    System.out.println(raf.getFilePointer());
}
  • Pointer ruleafter every read/write the pointer moves forward automatically; you go backward only via seek()
  • Usedatabases / record files with fixed-size records (jump to record n = n × recordSize), editing a byte in the middle of a file, appending
File Class — Files & Folders

java.io.File represents a path (file or directory) — not the content. Use it for information and management, streams for content.

File f = new File("data.txt");                   // or new File("C:/docs", "data.txt") / new File(dirObj, "x")
System.out.println(f.exists());                  // does it exist?
System.out.println(f.isFile() + " " + f.isDirectory());
System.out.println(f.getName() + " | " + f.getPath() + " | " + f.getAbsolutePath() + " | " + f.getParent());
System.out.println(f.length() + " bytes, modified " + new java.util.Date(f.lastModified()));
System.out.println(f.canRead() + " " + f.canWrite() + " " + f.canExecute());
f.createNewFile();  new File("logs").mkdir();  new File("a/b/c").mkdirs();
f.renameTo(new File("new.txt"));  f.delete();
f.setReadOnly();  f.setWritable(true);           // change permissions

File dir = new File(".");
for (String n : dir.list()) System.out.println(n);                 // names in the folder
File[] javaFiles = dir.listFiles((d, name) -> name.endsWith(".java"));   // FilenameFilter → only .java (or .txt) files
A read-only file opened for writing → FileNotFoundException: data.txt (Access is denied) — the message is misleading; change permission with setWritable(true).
  • Path separatorsuse / (works on all OS) or File.separator; escape \\ on Windows
  • Modern alternativejava.nio.file.Files / Path
Serialization — Storing Objects in Files

Problem: save the state of an object (a Student: roll no, name, marks) to a file and get it back later — as the same types, not as text.

AttemptHowDrawback
1) PrintStream / printlnwrite everything as textreading gives Strings → you must parse/type-cast each value; structure lost
2) DataOutputStream / DataInputStreamstore int as int, float as float, String as UTFyou must read in exactly the same order you wrote; you handle each field manually
3) Serialization — ObjectOutputStream / ObjectInputStreamstore the whole object✔ best: class must implement Serializable
Solution 2 – Data streamstry (DataOutputStream dos = new DataOutputStream(new FileOutputStream("s.dat"))) {
    dos.writeInt(101); dos.writeUTF("Ravi"); dos.writeFloat(85.5f);
}
try (DataInputStream dis = new DataInputStream(new FileInputStream("s.dat"))) {
    int roll = dis.readInt(); String name = dis.readUTF(); float marks = dis.readFloat();   // SAME ORDER
}
Solution 3 – Serializationclass Student implements Serializable {                       // marker interface (no methods)
    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;          // version id of the class
    int roll;  String name;
    transient String password;                                // NOT serialized (default value on read)
    static int count;                                         // static is NOT serialized (belongs to class)
    Student(int r, String n) { roll = r; name = n; }
}
// SERIALIZE (write object)
try (ObjectOutputStream oos = new ObjectOutputStream(new FileOutputStream("s.ser"))) {
    oos.writeObject(new Student(101, "Ravi"));
}
// DESERIALIZE (read object back)
try (ObjectInputStream ois = new ObjectInputStream(new FileInputStream("s.ser"))) {
    Student s = (Student) ois.readObject();                   // cast needed; may throw ClassNotFoundException
    System.out.println(s.roll + " " + s.name);
}
  • Rulesthe class must implement java.io.Serializable; all its non-transient fields must be serializable too (else NotSerializableException); the first non-serializable superclass must have a no-arg constructor
  • transientskip sensitive/temporary fields (passwords); static fields are never saved
  • Inside the streamObjectOutputStream also has writeInt, writeUTF etc. like DataOutputStream
  • serialVersionUIDif the class definition changes (constructor removed, fields added) between saving and loading with a different UID → InvalidClassException: local class incompatible; stream classdesc serialVersionUID = … → declare serialVersionUID explicitly to control compatibility
  • Orderread objects in the order they were written
Challenge: (1) serialize/deserialize a class; (2) store only float values with a DataOutputStream and read them back in the same format.
Saving game state, session data, caching objects, sending objects over a network (sockets), deep copy, RMI.
PrintStream & BufferedReader — Formatted Output & Line Input

PrintStream adds convenient print, println, printf, format methods (overloaded for all primitive types, char[], String, Object). System.out is a PrintStream object — a static member (out) of System, that's why you use it via the class name.

PrintStream ps = new PrintStream(new FileOutputStream("out.txt"));    // print INTO a file
ps.println("Roll: " + 101);
ps.printf("%d %s %.2f%n", 5, "Java", 3.14159);
ps.close();
System.setOut(ps);                       // redirect System.out to the file!
Reading line by line — BufferedReader (a Reader) + InputStreamReader bridge from System.inBufferedReader br = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(System.in));   // an InputStream cannot be assigned to a Reader → wrap it
String line = br.readLine();            // ALWAYS a String – null at end of input
int n = Integer.parseInt(br.readLine()); // parse yourself
Read a whole text filetry (BufferedReader br = new BufferedReader(new FileReader("data.txt"))) {
    String line;
    while ((line = br.readLine()) != null) System.out.println(line);
}
try (PrintWriter pw = new PrintWriter(new FileWriter("out.txt"))) { pw.println("Hello"); }
  • Scanner vs BufferedReaderScanner parses (nextInt()), BufferedReader is faster and returns Strings
  • Errors you may see*cannot find symbol* (missing import java.io.*) · unhandled IOException
Remember few classes and the pattern — Java is vast but easy to learn if you understand the pattern.
24

Generics

Lectures 24.1 – 24.2
The Problem Generics Solve

Before generics a collection or class had to store Object — any class object can be assigned to an Object reference — so type checking moved from compile time to run time.

Without genericsObject o = "Hi";
Object[] arr = { "Hi", "By", 25 };              // compiler accepts anything
for (Object x : arr) {
    String s = (String) x;                       // explicit cast needed to get it back in a String reference
    System.out.println(s);                       // "Hi", "By", then ✘ ClassCastException: Integer cannot be cast to String
}
  • Problemno compile-time check — the error appears at run time (ClassCastException)
  • Alsocasting everywhere makes code long and error-prone
  • Goal of generics"once I say I want to store a String, only Strings should be allowed" — errors caught by the compiler, no casting, reusable code
With genericsArrayList<String> list = new ArrayList<>();      // <String> = type argument
list.add("Hi");
// list.add(25);                                 // ✘ compile error: incompatible types: int cannot be converted to String
String s = list.get(0);                          // no cast
Generic Classes

A class with a type parameter (a placeholder for a type): class Name<T>. The type is given when an object is created.

class Data<T> {                                   // T = type parameter
    private T obj;                                // can hold ONE object of ANY type…
    void set(T obj) { this.obj = obj; }
    T get() { return obj; }
}
Data<String>  d1 = new Data<>();                  // <> diamond: type inferred (Java 7+)
d1.set("Java");   String s = d1.get();            // no cast
// d1.set(10);                                    // ✘ only String allowed
Data<Integer> d2 = new Data<>();  d2.set(10);     // for primitives use WRAPPER classes (int → Integer)
Data raw = new Data();                            // raw type → behaves as Data<Object> (avoid!)
Generic array holder & multiple parametersclass MyArray<T> {
    private T[] a;
    @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
    MyArray(int n) { a = (T[]) new Object[n]; }   // can't write new T[n]
    void set(int i, T v) { a[i] = v; }   T get(int i) { return a[i]; }
}
class Pair<K, V> {                                // two type parameters
    K key;  V value;
    Pair(K k, V v) { key = k; value = v; }
}
Pair<String, Integer> p = new Pair<>("age", 25);
LetterConvention
TType
EElement (collections)
K, VKey, Value (maps)
NNumber
S, U2nd, 3rd types
  • Only reference typesData<int> ✘ → Data<Integer> ✔ (autoboxing does the rest)
  • Default when nothing givenObject (raw type)
  • Extending a generic classclass MyArray2<T> extends MyArray<T> — the subclass must also be generic (or give a concrete type: class My3 extends MyArray<String>). Writing class My2 extends MyArray<T> without declaring T ✘
  • Not covariantData<Integer> is not a Data<Number> even though Integer is a Number
Bounded Types

Restrict which types may be used: <T extends Bound>.

class Stats<T extends Number> {                   // only Number and its subclasses (Integer, Double, …)
    T[] nums;
    Stats(T[] nums) { this.nums = nums; }
    double average() { double s = 0; for (T n : nums) s += n.doubleValue(); return s / nums.length; }
}
Stats<Integer> s1 = new Stats<>(new Integer[]{1, 2, 3});     // ✔
// Stats<String> s2 = ...                                     // ✘ String is not a Number

interface A { }
class Box<T extends A> { }                         // 'extends' is used for INTERFACES too – any class implementing A
// Box<String> b;                                  // ✘ String does not implement A

class Multi<T extends Person & A & B> { }          // multiple bounds: ONE class first, then interfaces (& separated)
// <T extends A & Person>  ✘ class must come first      <T extends Person & Student> ✘ only one class
  • Keywordonly extends is allowed in a generic class/interface definition (for both classes and interfaces); super is not allowed there
  • Bound order mirrors class rulesone superclass + many interfaces (a class can't inherit two classes, and extends comes before implements)
  • Only interfaces<T extends A & B> is fine
  • Benefitinside the class you may call the bound's methods (n.doubleValue())
Generic Methods & Wildcards
Generic method — declare <T> BEFORE the return typestatic <T> void show(T[] list) {                  // <T> declares the type variable for this method
    for (T x : list) System.out.print(x + " ");
    System.out.println();
}
show(new Integer[]{1, 2, 3});   show(new String[]{"a", "b"});      // T inferred from argument

static <T extends Comparable<T>> T max(T a, T b) { return a.compareTo(b) > 0 ? a : b; }
System.out.println(max(10, 20) + " " + max("apple", "pear"));      // 20 pear
  • statica method called from main must be static; static generic methods declare their own <T> (class T is not available in static context)
  • Works in a non-generic class too
Wildcards ?static void printAll(List<?> list)              { for (Object o : list) System.out.println(o); }   // unknown type – read only
static double sum(List<? extends Number> list)  { double s = 0; for (Number n : list) s += n.doubleValue(); return s; }   // upper bound
static void addInts(List<? super Integer> list) { list.add(10); }                                  // lower bound – can add Integers
WildcardMeaningUse
?any typeread/print any list
? extends TT or subclass (upper bound)producer — read from it (PECS: Producer Extends)
? super TT or superclass (lower bound)consumer — write into it (Consumer Super)
  • super is allowed in wildcards / method parameters but NOT in a class definition
Type erasure & restrictions// At compile time the compiler checks types, then ERASES them: Data<T> becomes Data with Object (or the bound).
// Consequences (not allowed):
// new T();  new T[10];  static T field;  x instanceof List<String>;  catch (T e);  Data<int>
Best practices: prefer List<String> over raw List; use bounded wildcards for flexible APIs; don't mix raw and generic types; use generic methods for utilities.

Collections Framework — List, Queue, Set, Map, Comparators & Utilities

Lectures 25.1 – 25.14
Why Collections? (Arrays Are Not Enough)
  • Array limitsfixed size; insert/delete in the middle means manual shifting; no built-in "unique" or "sorted" behaviour; only one kind of structure
  • Collections framework (java.util)ready-made data structures — dynamic size, rich methods, generic (<E>), hold objects only (wrappers for primitives via autoboxing)
  • Interfaces first, classes nextprogram to the interface (List<String> l = new ArrayList<>();)
HierarchyIterable
 └─ Collection
     ├─ List   ── ArrayList, LinkedList, Vector (→ Stack)
     ├─ Queue  ── PriorityQueue, LinkedList ── Deque ── ArrayDeque
     └─ Set    ── HashSet ── LinkedHashSet ;  SortedSet/NavigableSet ── TreeSet
Map (NOT a Collection): HashMap ── LinkedHashMap ; TreeMap (SortedMap) ; Hashtable ── Properties
Utilities: Collections, Arrays, Iterator, ListIterator, Comparable, Comparator
InterfaceOrdered?Duplicates?Special
Listinsertion order, index access✔positional
QueueFIFO (or priority)✔head/tail operations
SetHashSet none · LinkedHashSet insertion · TreeSet sorted✘ unique
Mapkey → valuekeys ✘ unique, values ✔lookup by key
Collection methodPurpose
add(e), addAll(c)insert
remove(o), removeAll(c), removeIf(pred), retainAll(c), clear()delete
contains(o), containsAll(c), isEmpty(), size()query
iterator(), forEach(consumer), stream()traversal
toArray()to array
ArrayList & LinkedList
ArrayList — dynamic arrayArrayList<Integer> list = new ArrayList<>();          // default capacity 10; grows ~1.5×
list.add(10); list.add(20); list.add(1, 15);          // add(index, value) – shifts the rest
list.set(0, 99);                                       // replace
list.remove(0);                                        // remove by INDEX (int)
list.remove(Integer.valueOf(20));                      // remove by VALUE (object)
System.out.println(list.get(0) + " " + list.size() + " " + list.indexOf(15) + " " + list.contains(15));
list.addAll(java.util.List.of(1, 2, 3));   list.sort(null);   // natural order
Integer[] arr = list.toArray(new Integer[0]);
List<Integer> part = list.subList(1, 3);
Traversal — four waysfor (int i = 0; i < list.size(); i++) System.out.print(list.get(i) + " ");     // index
for (int x : list) System.out.print(x + " ");                                   // for-each (auto-unboxing)
list.forEach(x -> System.out.print(x + " "));                                   // forEach + lambda
list.forEach(System.out::println);                                              // method reference
Iterator<Integer> it = list.iterator();
while (it.hasNext()) { int v = it.next(); if (v > 50) it.remove(); }            // safe removal while iterating
Collections + lambda: print only marks > 60ArrayList<Integer> marks = new ArrayList<>(java.util.List.of(45, 78, 62, 30));
marks.stream().filter(m -> m > 60).forEach(System.out::println);
marks.removeIf(m -> m < 40);
ArrayListLinkedListVector
Internalsresizable arraydoubly linked listresizable array, synchronized
get(i)O(1)O(n)O(1)
add/remove at ends / middleend O(1) amortised, middle O(n) (shifting)ends O(1), middle O(1) after reaching nodeO(n)
Memorycompactextra memory for links (prev/next)
Thread-safe✘✘✔ (legacy)
Alsoimplements List, RandomAccessimplements List and DequeStack extends Vector (legacy LIFO)
  • WrappersArrayList<int> ✘ → ArrayList<Integer>; list.remove(5) removes index 5 for List<Integer>
  • ConcurrentModificationExceptionmodifying a list inside a for-each loop → use Iterator.remove() or removeIf
ArrayList = default list (fast reads); LinkedList = frequent insert/delete at start/end, queues.
Queue, Deque, ArrayDeque & PriorityQueue

Queue = FIFO (first in, first out) like a ticket counter. Deque = double-ended queue (insert/remove at both ends) — also gives stack (LIFO) behaviour.

OperationThrows exception if it failsReturns special value
insert (tail)add(e)offer(e) → false
remove headremove()poll() → null
examine headelement()peek() → null
Deque endsaddFirst/addLast/removeFirst/removeLast/getFirst/getLastofferFirst/offerLast/pollFirst/pollLast/peekFirst/peekLast
Stack ops on Dequepush(e) = addFirst · pop() = removeFirst
Queue<String> q = new LinkedList<>();
q.offer("A"); q.offer("B"); q.offer("C");
System.out.println(q.peek());          // A
System.out.println(q.poll() + " " + q);// A [B, C]

Deque<Integer> dq = new ArrayDeque<>();         // circular array – faster than LinkedList & Stack; no null elements
dq.addFirst(1); dq.addLast(2); dq.push(0);      // [0, 1, 2]
System.out.println(dq.pollLast() + " " + dq.pop());   // 2 0
PriorityQueue — element with highest PRIORITY comes out first (a heap, not FIFO)PriorityQueue<Integer> pq = new PriorityQueue<>();               // MIN-heap: smallest first (natural order)
pq.addAll(java.util.List.of(40, 10, 30, 20));
System.out.println(pq.poll() + " " + pq.poll());                 // 10 20   (printing pq itself shows heap order, not sorted!)

PriorityQueue<Integer> maxHeap = new PriorityQueue<>(Comparator.reverseOrder());   // largest first
PriorityQueue<Task> tasks = new PriorityQueue<>((a, b) -> a.priority - b.priority);  // custom comparator
  • Comparator resultnegative → first argument has higher priority (comes first), 0 → equal, positive → second first
  • No nulls in PriorityQueue/ArrayDeque; elements must be Comparable or supply a Comparator
Task schedulers, print queues, BFS graph traversal, undo stacks (Deque), top-k problems (PriorityQueue).
Hashing — How HashSet & HashMap Work

Hashing = compute an array index from the data itself, so search/insert/delete are almost O(1) — no scanning.

  • Hash tablearray of buckets. Hash function e.g. index = hashCode % tableSize
  • Collisiontwo keys map to the same index. Handling: linear probing (open addressing — put it in the next free slot; searching stops at a blank slot) or chaining (each slot holds a list). Java's HashMap uses chaining (buckets become linked lists / balanced trees when long)
  • Load factorelements / capacity — default 0.75 (table is ~75 % full then it rehashes: doubles capacity and re-inserts). Lower factor = faster but more memory; higher = compact but more collisions
  • Initial capacitydefault 16 (new HashSet<>(capacity, loadFactor))
  • Contractequal objects must have equal hashCode() — override both equals and hashCode for your classes used as keys
class Point {
    int x, y;
    Point(int x, int y) { this.x = x; this.y = y; }
    @Override public boolean equals(Object o) { return o instanceof Point p && p.x == x && p.y == y; }
    @Override public int hashCode() { return java.util.Objects.hash(x, y); }
}
HashSet<Point> s = new HashSet<>();  s.add(new Point(1, 2));  s.add(new Point(1, 2));
System.out.println(s.size());        // 1 (duplicate detected through hashCode+equals)
Set — HashSet, LinkedHashSet, TreeSet
HashSetLinkedHashSetTreeSet
Ordernone (hash order)insertion ordersorted (natural or Comparator)
Structurehash tablehash table + doubly linked listred-black tree (self-balancing)
SpeedO(1)O(1)O(log n)

Set<String> hs = new HashSet<>(java.util.List.of("pen", "book", "pen", "bag"));   // duplicates dropped
Set<Integer> ts = new TreeSet<>(java.util.List.of(50, 10, 40, 20));
System.out.println(ts);                                     // [10, 20, 40, 50]  ← sorted

TreeSet<Integer> t = new TreeSet<>(ts);
System.out.println(t.first() + " " + t.last());            // 10 50
System.out.println(t.floor(25) + " " + t.ceiling(25));     // 20 40   (≤25 and ≥25)
System.out.println(t.lower(20) + " " + t.higher(20));      // 10 40   (strictly < and >)
System.out.println(t.headSet(40) + " " + t.tailSet(40) + " " + t.subSet(10, 40));   // [10,20] [40,50] [10,20]
System.out.println(t.descendingSet() + " " + t.pollFirst());
Set operationsa.addAll(b);      // union
a.retainAll(b);   // intersection
a.removeAll(b);   // difference
a.containsAll(b); // subset?
A TreeSet of your own class throws ClassCastException unless the class implements Comparable (or you pass a Comparator).
Comparable vs Comparator

Sorting needs a rule for "which comes first".

ComparableComparator
Package / methodjava.lang — int compareTo(T o)java.util — int compare(T a, T b)
Where the rule livesinside the class (natural ordering — one only)outside — as many rules as you like
Used byTreeSet, TreeMap, Collections.sort(list), Arrays.sortsame, passed as an argument
Returnnegative: this < other · 0 equal · positive: this > othernegative: a first · 0 · positive: b first
class Student implements Comparable<Student> {
    int roll;  String name;  double marks;
    Student(int r, String n, double m) { roll = r; name = n; marks = m; }
    @Override public int compareTo(Student o) { return Integer.compare(roll, o.roll); }   // natural = by roll
    @Override public String toString() { return roll + " " + name + " " + marks; }
}
List<Student> list = new ArrayList<>();
Collections.sort(list);                                              // by roll (natural order)
Collections.sort(list, (a, b) -> a.name.compareTo(b.name));          // by name  (Comparator lambda)
list.sort(Comparator.comparingDouble((Student s) -> s.marks).reversed());   // by marks desc
list.sort(Comparator.comparing((Student s) -> s.name).thenComparingInt(s -> s.roll));   // name then roll
TreeSet<Student> byName = new TreeSet<>(Comparator.comparing(s -> s.name));
  • Comparator as separate classclass ByMarks implements Comparator<Student> { public int compare(Student a, Student b) { … } }
  • toStringoverride it so printing a collection shows readable objects
  • Descendingswap the two operands or use .reversed() / Comparator.reverseOrder()
Map — HashMap, LinkedHashMap, TreeMap, Hashtable

Key → value pairs. Keys are unique; a duplicate key replaces the old value. Not part of Collection.

Map<String, Integer> m = new HashMap<>();
m.put("Ravi", 80);  m.put("Anu", 90);  m.put("Ravi", 85);      // replaces Ravi's value
System.out.println(m.get("Ravi") + " " + m.getOrDefault("Zed", 0));   // 85 0
m.putIfAbsent("Kim", 70);
m.merge("Anu", 5, Integer::sum);                              // add to existing value → 95
System.out.println(m.containsKey("Kim") + " " + m.containsValue(70) + " " + m.size());
m.remove("Kim");

for (String k : m.keySet()) System.out.println(k + " → " + m.get(k));
for (Map.Entry<String, Integer> e : m.entrySet()) System.out.println(e.getKey() + "=" + e.getValue());
m.forEach((k, v) -> System.out.println(k + " " + v));
Collection<Integer> vals = m.values();
HashMapLinkedHashMapTreeMapHashtable
Ordernoneinsertion (or access order)sorted by keynone
SpeedO(1)O(1)O(log n)O(1)
null key / value1 null key, many null valuessame✘ null key✘ none
Thread-safe✘✘✘✔ synchronized (legacy)
Structurehash table (buckets → tree)+ linked listred-black treehash table
TreeMap — navigation methodsTreeMap<Integer, String> tm = new TreeMap<>();
tm.put(10, "A"); tm.put(30, "C"); tm.put(20, "B");
System.out.println(tm.firstKey() + " " + tm.lastEntry());          // 10  30=C
System.out.println(tm.floorKey(25) + " " + tm.ceilingKey(25));     // 20 30
System.out.println(tm.lowerEntry(20) + " " + tm.higherEntry(20));  // 10=A 30=C
System.out.println(tm.headMap(30) + " " + tm.tailMap(20) + " " + tm.descendingMap());
LinkedHashMap as an LRU cache (removes least recently used)class Cache<K, V> extends LinkedHashMap<K, V> {
    private final int max;
    Cache(int max) { super(16, 0.75f, true); this.max = max; }     // true = ACCESS order
    @Override protected boolean removeEldestEntry(Map.Entry<K, V> eldest) { return size() > max; }
}
  • Counting wordsfor (String w : words) map.merge(w, 1, Integer::sum);
  • Keys must implement hashCode/equals (HashMap) or be comparable (TreeMap)
  • Hashtable vs HashMapHashtable is old, synchronized, no nulls; use HashMap (or ConcurrentHashMap)

while (st.hasMoreTokens()) System.out.print(st.nextToken() + " ");   // a b c
System.out.println(st.countTokens());
BitSet — a compact array of bits (true/false flags)BitSet bs = new BitSet(16);
bs.set(1); bs.set(5); bs.set(9);
System.out.println(bs.get(5) + " " + bs.cardinality() + " " + bs.nextSetBit(2) + " " + bs);   // true 3 5 {1, 5, 9}
bs.flip(1); bs.clear(9);
BitSet b2 = new BitSet(); b2.set(5);  bs.and(b2);      // also or(), xor(), andNot()
Student Project 25.10 — Menu-Driven Bank Accounts with HashMap + File Persistence
Store many Account objects in a HashMap<String, Account> keyed by account number; keep them between runs by serializing the map to a file. Menu: 1 create, 2 view all, 3 view one, 4 deposit, 5 withdraw, 6 delete, 7 exit & save. On start-up check the file exists (new File(...).exists()) before reading — otherwise FileNotFoundException the first time.
class Account implements Serializable {
    private String accNo, name;  private double balance;
    Account(String no, String n, double b) { accNo = no; name = n; balance = b; }
    void deposit(double a)  { balance += a; }
    boolean withdraw(double a) { if (a > balance) return false; balance -= a; return true; }
    public String toString() { return accNo + " " + name + " " + balance; }
}
public class Bank {
    static Map<String, Account> accounts = new HashMap<>();
    @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
    static void load() throws Exception {
        File f = new File("accounts.dat");
        if (!f.exists()) return;                                   // first run – nothing to load
        try (ObjectInputStream in = new ObjectInputStream(new FileInputStream(f))) {
            accounts = (Map<String, Account>) in.readObject();
        }
    }
    static void save() throws Exception {
        try (ObjectOutputStream out = new ObjectOutputStream(new FileOutputStream("accounts.dat"))) {
            out.writeObject(accounts);                              // HashMap itself is Serializable
        }
    }
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        load();
        Scanner sc = new Scanner(System.in);
        int ch;
        do {
            System.out.println("1 Create 2 View all 3 View 4 Deposit 5 Withdraw 6 Delete 7 Exit");
            ch = sc.nextInt();
            switch (ch) {
                case 1: { System.out.print("no name bal: "); String no = sc.next(), n = sc.next(); double b = sc.nextDouble();
                          accounts.put(no, new Account(no, n, b)); break; }
                case 2: accounts.values().forEach(System.out::println); break;
                case 3: System.out.println(accounts.get(sc.next())); break;
                case 4: { Account a = accounts.get(sc.next()); if (a != null) a.deposit(sc.nextDouble()); break; }
                case 5: { Account a = accounts.get(sc.next()); if (a != null && !a.withdraw(sc.nextDouble())) System.out.println("Insufficient"); break; }
                case 6: accounts.remove(sc.next()); break;
            }
        } while (ch != 7);
        save();
    }
}
Map lookup by ID is the core of caches, inventories, user sessions, word-frequency counters and in-memory databases.

Date & Time API

Lectures 26.1 – 26.2
Legacy Date Classes vs java.time

Java has two generations of date/time APIs. Use java.time (Java 8+) in new code; know the legacy ones because you will meet them.

Legacy (java.util)Modern (java.time)
ClassesDate, Calendar, GregorianCalendar, TimeZone, SimpleDateFormatLocalDate, LocalTime, LocalDateTime, ZonedDateTime, Instant, Period, Duration…
Mutable?mutable (not thread-safe)immutable — methods return a new object
Year / monthyear counted from 1900, months 0–11 (Jan = 0)real values: year 2025, month 1–12 (or Month.JANUARY)
Time zonemessy (Date has no zone, Calendar does)separate classes per need
Designone class does everythingone small class for each need
Statusmany methods deprecatedrecommended (inspired by Joda-Time)
LegacyDate d = new Date();                          // now – milliseconds since the epoch (1 Jan 1970 00:00 UTC)
Date old = new Date(80, 0, 1);                // ✘ deprecated: year = 1900 + 80, month 0 = January
System.out.println(d.getTime());              // ms since epoch
Calendar c = Calendar.getInstance();
c.set(2025, Calendar.JANUARY, 15);
c.add(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH, 10);
System.out.println(c.get(Calendar.YEAR) + " " + (c.get(Calendar.MONTH) + 1));
TimeZone tz = TimeZone.getTimeZone("Asia/Kolkata");        // India is UTC+5:30
  • Joda-Timea popular third-party library that fixed the legacy problems; its design became java.time (JSR-310) in Java 8. Old Joda code maps almost 1-to-1 to java.time
  • Epoch1970-01-01T00:00:00Z — the reference point of machine time
  • Warning you'll see*"Date(int,int,int) in Date has been deprecated"* → move to java.time
java.time Classes — Which One?
ClassHoldsExample
LocalDatedate only (year-month-day), no time, no zone2025-06-15
LocalTimetime only (hour-min-sec-nano)17:30:00
LocalDateTimedate + time, no zone2025-06-15T17:30
ZonedDateTimedate + time + zone ID (DST aware)2025-06-15T17:30+05:30[Asia/Kolkata]
OffsetDateTime / OffsetTimedate-time with only a UTC offset (+05:30)
Instanta moment on the timeline (machine time, UTC; same value everywhere on earth)2025-06-15T12:00:00Z
Year, YearMonth, MonthDayonly year / year+month / month+day (birthday, credit-card expiry)Year.of(2024).isLeap()
Periodamount of time in date units (years, months, days)2 years 3 months 5 days
Durationamount of time in time units (hours, minutes, seconds)4 h 30 m
ZoneId / ZoneOffsettime zoneZoneId.of("Asia/Kolkata")
Clocksource of current timeClock.systemDefaultZone()
DayOfWeek, MonthenumsDayOfWeek.MONDAY
  • A separate class for each minor differencechoose the smallest class that carries the information you need
  • Only what's stored is availablea LocalDate object has no hour methods; YearMonth has no day methods
  • Instant vs localtwo Instants created at the same moment in two countries have the same value; local classes depend on the local clock
Creating Date/Time Objects (No new — Static Factory Methods)
LocalDate today  = LocalDate.now();                      // system clock, default zone
LocalDate d1     = LocalDate.of(2025, 6, 15);            // year, month, day  (or Month.JUNE)
LocalDate d2     = LocalDate.ofEpochDay(1);              // day 1 after 1970-01-01 → 1970-01-02
LocalDate d3     = LocalDate.ofYearDay(2025, 100);       // 100th day of the year
LocalDate d4     = LocalDate.parse("2020-01-01");        // ISO format only: yyyy-MM-dd
LocalDate d5     = LocalDate.now(ZoneId.of("Asia/Kolkata"));
LocalDate d6     = LocalDate.now(Clock.systemDefaultZone());

LocalTime t1 = LocalTime.now();   LocalTime t2 = LocalTime.of(17, 30);   LocalTime t3 = LocalTime.parse("17:30:15");
LocalDateTime dt = LocalDateTime.of(2025, 6, 15, 17, 30);   // or  d1.atTime(t2)
ZonedDateTime z  = ZonedDateTime.now(ZoneId.of("America/New_York"));
Instant i = Instant.now();   Instant e = Instant.ofEpochSecond(0);   // 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z
LocalDate.parse("2020-1-5") ✘ DateTimeParseException — month and day must be two digits (2020-01-05), separator is a hyphen, order is year-month-day. ZoneId.of("Asia/Kolkatta") (spelling mistake) → ZoneRulesException.
NoteDetail
5 PMis stored as 17 (24-hour clock)
Month numbers1–12 (unlike Calendar)
now()takes no arguments, or a ZoneId / Clock
of(...)arguments must be valid → LocalDate.of(2025, 2, 30) throws DateTimeException
Reading, Changing & Comparing (Immutable!)
LocalDate d = LocalDate.of(2025, 6, 15);
System.out.println(d.getYear() + " " + d.getMonth() + " " + d.getMonthValue() + " " + d.getDayOfMonth()
                   + " " + d.getDayOfWeek() + " " + d.getDayOfYear() + " " + d.isLeapYear() + " " + d.lengthOfMonth());

LocalDate next = d.plusDays(10);            // returns a NEW object – d itself never changes
d.plusMonths(1);                            // result lost unless assigned!
LocalDate prev = d.minusYears(1).minusWeeks(2);
LocalDate first = d.withDayOfMonth(1);      // with… = replace one field (new object)
LocalDate adj  = d.with(java.time.temporal.TemporalAdjusters.lastDayOfMonth());

LocalDateTime dt = LocalDateTime.of(2025, 6, 15, 17, 30);
dt = dt.plusHours(5).minusMinutes(10);
LocalTime lt = dt.toLocalTime();  LocalDate ld = dt.toLocalDate();

System.out.println(d.isBefore(next) + " " + d.isAfter(next) + " " + d.equals(LocalDate.of(2025, 6, 15)) + " " + d.compareTo(next));
DifferencesLocalDate a = LocalDate.of(2000, 1, 15), b = LocalDate.of(2025, 6, 20);
Period p = Period.between(a, b);                            // 25 years 5 months 5 days
System.out.println(p.getYears() + "y " + p.getMonths() + "m " + p.getDays() + "d");
long days = java.time.temporal.ChronoUnit.DAYS.between(a, b);   // total days
Duration du = Duration.between(LocalTime.of(9, 0), LocalTime.of(13, 30));
System.out.println(du.toHours() + " h " + du.toMinutesPart() + " m");       // 4 h 30 m
Duration run = Duration.ofHours(4).plusMinutes(30);        // "a program running for 4 hours 30 minutes"
Period pp = Period.of(2, 3, 5);                             // 2 years, 3 months, 5 days
  • Immutableno setters; every plus/minus/with returns a new object → "same sense as String"
  • plus/minus methodsare the only way to "modify" time values
  • Age calculatorPeriod.between(birthDate, LocalDate.now()).getYears()
Age, due dates, deadlines/timers, booking systems, time-zone conversion for meetings, logging timestamps (Instant).

ZoneId india = ZoneId.of("Asia/Kolkata");   ZoneId ny = ZoneId.of("America/New_York");
ZonedDateTime inIndia = ZonedDateTime.now(india);

  • IndiaUTC+05:30 ("India is five and a half hours ahead of UTC"); TZ = time zone
  • Zone IDregion names like Asia/Kolkata (avoid 3-letter IDs such as IST)
  • DSTZonedDateTime applies daylight-saving rules automatically
DateTimeFormatter — Formatting & Parsing

Display date/time the way you like, and read text into date objects. Immutable & thread-safe (unlike SimpleDateFormat). You cannot new it — use ofPattern(...) or a predefined constant.

SymbolMeaningExample
yyyy / yyyear2025 / 25
M MM MMM MMMMupper-case M = month6, 06, Jun, June
d ddday of month5, 05
E / EEEEday of weekSun / Sunday
H HHhour 0–2317
h hhhour 1–125
m mmlower-case m = minutes30
s ssseconds45
S / SSSfraction of second (ms)123
aAM / PMPM
z Z XXXzone name / offsetIST, +0530, +05:30
LocalDateTime dt = LocalDateTime.of(2025, 6, 15, 17, 30, 45);

DateTimeFormatter f1 = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("dd/MM/yyyy");
System.out.println(dt.format(f1));                                           // 15/06/2025   (two D's → two digits)
System.out.println(f1.format(dt));                                          // same
System.out.println(dt.format(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("dd MMM yyyy, hh:mm a")));   // 15 Jun 2025, 05:30 PM
System.out.println(dt.format(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("EEEE, MMMM d, yyyy HH:mm:ss")));
System.out.println(dt.format(DateTimeFormatter.ISO_LOCAL_DATE_TIME));       // 2025-06-15T17:30:45
System.out.println(dt.format(DateTimeFormatter.ofLocalizedDate(java.time.format.FormatStyle.MEDIUM)));

LocalDate parsed = LocalDate.parse("15/06/2025", f1);                       // text → date using the same pattern
LocalDateTime p2 = LocalDateTime.parse("2025-06-15 17:30", DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm"));
Field must exist in the object: formatting a LocalDate with a time pattern (HH:mm) → UnsupportedTemporalTypeException: Unsupported field: HourOfDay (message: *unable to extract value*). Likewise you cannot get zone information from a LocalDateTime. Use ZonedDateTime for zone fields.
Reading a field by ChronoFieldint hour = dt.get(java.time.temporal.ChronoField.HOUR_OF_DAY);             // only fields present in that object
long epochDay = LocalDate.of(2025,6,15).getLong(java.time.temporal.ChronoField.EPOCH_DAY);
M = Month, m = minutes — the classic pattern typo. yyyy-MM-dd ✔ vs yyyy-mm-dd ✘.

Network Programming — Sockets, TCP & UDP

Lectures 27.1 – 27.4
Networking Concepts You Need First
  • Networkcomputers connected to exchange data. Client in India ↔ server in USA are not directly connected — data hops through many machines/routers
  • Client–serverthe server waits for requests (always running, in an infinite loop — like Google's web server); the client starts communication
  • Layered model (OSI)each layer talks to the same layer on the other machine (via a protocol = a set of agreed "dialogues") and to the adjacent layers on its own machine
LayerJob
7 Applicationuser-level protocols — HTTP, FTP, SMTP, DNS
6 Presentationencoding, encryption, compression
5 Sessionopens, manages and closes sessions
4 Transportend-to-end delivery — TCP / UDP, ports
3 Networkaddressing & routing — IP
2 Data linkframes between adjacent devices (MAC)
1 Physicalcables, radio waves, bits
  • IP addressidentifies a machine. IPv4 = 4 bytes (192.168.1.10) — not enough addresses → IPv6 = 16 bytes (128-bit)
  • Port16-bit number (0–65535) identifying which application on the machine; several servers can run on one computer, each on its own port (HTTP 80, HTTPS 443, FTP 21, SMTP 25)
  • localhost / 127.0.0.1this machine only
  • SocketIP address + port number — one endpoint of a two-way connection
TCPUDP
Typeconnection-oriented, reliableconnectionless, unreliable
Deliveryguaranteed, in order, error-checkedpackets may be lost / duplicated / reordered
Speedslower (handshake, acks)fast, low overhead
Unitstream of bytesdatagram packets
Java classesSocket, ServerSocketDatagramSocket, DatagramPacket
Used forweb (HTTP), email, file transferstreaming audio/video, games, DNS — lost packets are acceptable
Handy classes in java.netInetAddress ip = InetAddress.getByName("www.google.com");
System.out.println(ip.getHostAddress() + " " + InetAddress.getLocalHost());
URL u = new URL("https://www.example.com:443/docs/index.html?x=1");
System.out.println(u.getProtocol() + " " + u.getHost() + " " + u.getPort() + " " + u.getPath() + " " + u.getQuery());
TCP Echo Server & Client (Reverse Echo)

Echo server = returns what the client sends. Here it sends the message back reversed — the classic first client–server program. Two programs: Server (run first) and Client.

1

Server: new ServerSocket(port)

opens the port (needs only the port number; may throw IOException → try/catch or throws Exception)

2

Server: accept()

blocks until a client connects, returns a Socket for that client

3

Streams

socket.getInputStream() / getOutputStream() — wrap in BufferedReader / PrintWriter (or Scanner)

4

Client: new Socket("localhost", port)

connects (host = server machine; localhost when both run on same computer)

5

Exchange messages

read/write lines; server processes, replies

6

Close everything

socket.close() — a socket is a very precious resource; always close it when finished

ReverseEchoServer.javaimport java.io.*;  import java.net.*;
public class ReverseEchoServer {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        ServerSocket ss = new ServerSocket(5000);                 // 1) listen on port 5000
        System.out.println("Server started, waiting for client…");
        Socket s = ss.accept();                                    // 2) wait for a client
        BufferedReader in  = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(s.getInputStream()));
        PrintWriter    out = new PrintWriter(s.getOutputStream(), true);       // true = autoflush
        String msg;
        while ((msg = in.readLine()) != null) {                    // null → client closed
            if (msg.equalsIgnoreCase("bye")) break;
            out.println(new StringBuilder(msg).reverse());         // reverse and send back
        }
        s.close();  ss.close();                                    // always close
    }
}
ReverseEchoClient.java  (run in a second console)import java.io.*;  import java.net.*;  import java.util.Scanner;
public class ReverseEchoClient {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        Socket s = new Socket("localhost", 5000);                  // same machine
        BufferedReader in  = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(s.getInputStream()));
        PrintWriter    out = new PrintWriter(s.getOutputStream(), true);
        Scanner sc = new Scanner(System.in);
        String msg;
        while (true) {
            System.out.print("Enter message: ");
            msg = sc.nextLine();                                   // user types on keyboard
            out.println(msg);
            if (msg.equalsIgnoreCase("bye")) break;
            System.out.println("Server says: " + in.readLine());
        }
        s.close();
    }
}
OutputEnter message: hello
Server says: olleh
  • Which is whichserver program starts first, client after; use the same port
  • Port already in useBindException: Address already in use — another server holds that port
  • Connection refusedserver not running / wrong host or port
  • Blocking callsaccept() and readLine() wait; that is why the next lecture uses threads
Multithreaded Server — Many Clients at the Same Time

The single-client server serves one client at a time. For a multi-user server: the main thread only accepts connections; each accepted Socket is handed to a new thread that talks to that client.

class ReverseEchoThread extends Thread {                            // (or implements Runnable)
    private Socket s;
    ReverseEchoThread(Socket s) { this.s = s; }                     // constructor receives the client's socket
    @Override public void run() {                                   // run() cannot say 'throws' → use try/catch inside
        try (BufferedReader in = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(s.getInputStream()));
             PrintWriter out = new PrintWriter(s.getOutputStream(), true)) {
            String msg;
            while ((msg = in.readLine()) != null && !msg.equalsIgnoreCase("bye"))
                out.println(new StringBuilder(msg).reverse());
        } catch (IOException e) { System.out.println("Client error: " + e.getMessage()); }
        finally { try { s.close(); } catch (IOException e) { } }
    }
}
public class MultiServer {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        ServerSocket ss = new ServerSocket(5000);
        System.out.println("Server has started");
        while (true) {                                              // servers run in an infinite loop (never stop by themselves)
            Socket s = ss.accept();                                 // blocks until a client arrives
            new ReverseEchoThread(s).start();                       // hand over to a thread, go back to accept()
        }
        // no ss.close() – this line is unreachable (loop never ends)
    }
}
  • Designone thread per client (or use ExecutorService thread pool for many clients)
  • Same client programrun several client consoles simultaneously — each gets its own thread
  • Shared stateif threads modify common data → synchronize (see Multithreading)
Chat servers, web servers, multiplayer game backends, remote command execution.
UDP — Datagram Communication

UDP = User Datagram Protocol. Connectionless: no accept(), no handshake — just send packets to an IP + port. Classes in java.net: DatagramSocket (send/receive) and DatagramPacket (data + address + port).

Receiver (server) — start firstimport java.net.*;
public class UdpServer {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        DatagramSocket ds = new DatagramSocket(4000);               // listen on port 4000
        byte[] buf = new byte[1024];
        DatagramPacket p = new DatagramPacket(buf, buf.length);     // empty packet to fill
        ds.receive(p);                                              // blocks until a packet arrives
        String msg = new String(p.getData(), 0, p.getLength());    // use real length (or .trim())
        System.out.println("Received: " + msg + " from " + p.getAddress() + ":" + p.getPort());
        ds.close();
    }
}
Sender (client)public class UdpClient {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
        DatagramSocket ds = new DatagramSocket();
        byte[] data = "Hello UDP".getBytes();                       // String.getBytes() → byte[]
        InetAddress ip = InetAddress.getByName("localhost");        // same machine for testing
        DatagramPacket p = new DatagramPacket(data, data.length, ip, 4000);   // data, LENGTH of message, address, port
        ds.send(p);
        ds.close();
    }
}
  • Give lengtha packet needs the byte array and its length; when receiving, the buffer is larger than the message → use p.getLength() or trim() to remove padding
  • Two public classes can't be in one fileserver and client go in separate files (or make one non-public)
  • Loss is acceptablestreaming video/audio — a lost packet is simply skipped
  • ErrorsSocketException, IOException → handle or throws Exception
Live video/voice calls, online games (positions), DNS lookups, sensor broadcasts.
28

Databases & JDBC (SQL essentials + java.sql)

Lectures 28.1 – 28.6
Database Basics & Terminology

A database stores data permanently in an organised form. Files alone are problematic: you must know the format (write roll number first → you must read roll number first), searching/sharing/security is manual. A DBMS (SQLite, MySQL, Oracle, PostgreSQL, SQL Server) manages data and speaks SQL.

TermMeaning
Table (relation)data arranged in rows and columns — Students, Departments
Row / record / tupleone entry (one student)
Column / field / attributeone property (name, city, deptno)
Data typesINTEGER, TEXT/VARCHAR, REAL/FLOAT, DATE… — differ from Java's types, so JDBC converts
NULL"value not present" (not zero, not empty)
Primary keycolumn(s) that uniquely identify a row — unique + not null (rollno) → duplicate 15 cannot be inserted
Foreign keycolumn that must match a primary key in another table (student.deptno → department.deptno) → referential integrity: you can't insert dept 60 if it doesn't exist (only 10–50 exist)
ConstraintsNOT NULL, UNIQUE, PRIMARY KEY, FOREIGN KEY, CHECK, DEFAULT
Images/large filesstore a reference/path (or BLOB)
  • Example schemaStudents(rollno, name, city, deptno); Departments(deptno, dname) where 10 = Computer, 30 = Civil, 40 = Mechanical…
  • SQL statement familiesDDL (Data Definition) · DML (Data Manipulation) · DQL (Query) · TCL (Transactions)
SQL Basics — DDL & DML
FamilyCommandsPurpose
DDLCREATE, ALTER, DROPdefine structure — used once after designing the database; then rarely changed
DMLINSERT, UPDATE, DELETEchange the data
DQLSELECTretrieve data (query = data-retrieval language)
TCLCOMMIT, ROLLBACK, SAVEPOINTtransactions
DDLCREATE TABLE departments (
    deptno INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    dname  TEXT NOT NULL
);
CREATE TABLE students (
    rollno INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,           -- unique + not null
    name   TEXT NOT NULL,
    city   TEXT,
    deptno INTEGER REFERENCES departments(deptno)   -- foreign key: value must exist in departments
);
ALTER TABLE students ADD COLUMN email TEXT;
DROP TABLE students;
DMLINSERT INTO departments VALUES (10, 'Computer');
INSERT INTO students (rollno, name, city, deptno) VALUES (16, 'Smith', 'Delhi', 10);
UPDATE students SET city = 'Mumbai' WHERE rollno = 16;
DELETE FROM students WHERE rollno = 16;
-- INSERT with rollno 15 again → ERROR: UNIQUE constraint failed (primary key)
-- INSERT with deptno 60 → ERROR: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed (60 not in departments)
Forget the WHERE in UPDATE/DELETE and every row is changed/deleted.
SQL Queries — SELECT, WHERE, JOIN, GROUP BY
SELECT * FROM students;                                   -- all columns; SELECT needs FROM
SELECT name, city FROM students;
SELECT DISTINCT deptno FROM students;                     -- remove duplicates
SELECT * FROM students WHERE deptno = 40 AND city = 'Delhi';   -- AND: both must be true; OR: any; NOT
SELECT * FROM students WHERE name LIKE '%y';              -- % = any number of chars, _ = exactly one char → names ending in y
SELECT * FROM students WHERE deptno IN (10, 20) AND rollno BETWEEN 5 AND 12;
SELECT * FROM students WHERE city IS NULL;                -- NULL needs IS NULL, not = NULL
SELECT * FROM students ORDER BY name DESC;                -- ASC default

SELECT s.name, d.dname                                    -- JOIN: match rows having the same key
FROM students s JOIN departments d ON s.deptno = d.deptno;    -- must say ON which column (without it → cartesian product = every row × every row)

SELECT deptno, COUNT(*) AS total, AVG(marks) FROM students
GROUP BY deptno                                           -- one result row per group
HAVING COUNT(*) > 3;                                      -- HAVING filters groups (WHERE filters rows)
AggregateMeaning
COUNT(*) / COUNT(col) / COUNT(DISTINCT col)rows / non-null values / unique values (e.g. 14)
SUM(col) AVG(col) MIN(col) MAX(col)numeric summaries — used with SELECT
Advanced SQL — Subqueries & Set Operations
Subquery = a query inside another querySELECT name FROM students
WHERE deptno = (SELECT deptno FROM departments WHERE dname = 'Computer');   -- '=' needs ONE value; inner SELECT must return only deptno

SELECT name FROM students
WHERE deptno IN (SELECT deptno FROM departments WHERE dname LIKE 'C%');       -- IN for many values

SELECT * FROM students WHERE marks > (SELECT AVG(marks) FROM students);
Set operations — both SELECTs must have the same number & compatible types of columnsSELECT name FROM students WHERE city = 'Delhi'
UNION                                                     -- combine, common rows appear ONCE (UNION ALL keeps duplicates)
SELECT name FROM students WHERE deptno = 10;

SELECT name FROM a INTERSECT SELECT name FROM b;          -- common rows only
SELECT name FROM a EXCEPT    SELECT name FROM b;          -- in a but not b (MINUS in Oracle)
Same query pattern every time — only the table and column names change.
JDBC Architecture & Overview

JDBC (Java Database Connectivity) = Java API (package java.sql) to talk to any relational database in SQL. Java and databases have different data types, so a driver translates between them.

Java Program ──► JDBC API (java.sql) ──► DriverManager ──► JDBC Driver (vendor-specific .jar) ──► Database
                      Connection · Statement · PreparedStatement · ResultSet
  • Drivera database-specific Java library (SQLite: sqlite-jdbc.jar, MySQL: mysql-connector-j, Oracle: ojdbc). Add it to the classpath. A driver for one database can't connect to another — and it must be Java code (a Python driver won't work in Java)
  • Everything talks SQLyour Java program sends SQL text to the database
Type / ClassRole
DriverManagercreates connections (registered drivers)
Connection (interface)session with the database
Statementrun a plain SQL string
PreparedStatementprecompiled SQL with ? parameters
CallableStatementcall stored procedures
ResultSettable of rows returned by a query (cursor)
SQLExceptionchecked exception for all DB errors
First JDBC Program — Steps
1

Load the driver

Class.forName("org.sqlite.JDBC"); — creates the driver class object on the fly (nameless); it registers itself with DriverManager. Class name must be exact (documented by the driver). Not needed with JDBC 4+/modern drivers on the classpath

2

Connect

Connection con = DriverManager.getConnection(url); — SQLite: "jdbc:sqlite:mydb.db"; MySQL: "jdbc:mysql://localhost:3306/school", user, pass

3

Create statement

Statement st = con.createStatement();

4

Execute SQL

st.executeQuery(sql) for SELECT → ResultSet; st.executeUpdate(sql) for INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/DDL → row count

5

Process results

while (rs.next()) { rs.getInt("rollno"); rs.getString("name"); }

6

Release resources

close ResultSet, Statement, Connection (resources must be released) — or try-with-resources

import java.sql.*;
public class Demo {
    public static void main(String[] args) {          // 'throws Exception' also works for quick demos
        String url = "jdbc:sqlite:school.db";
        try (Connection con = DriverManager.getConnection(url);
             Statement st = con.createStatement();
             ResultSet rs = st.executeQuery("SELECT rollno, name, city FROM students")) {

            while (rs.next()) {                         // next() → true while another record exists; cursor starts BEFORE the first row
                int roll = rs.getInt("rollno");         // by column name … or rs.getInt(1) by position (starts at 1)
                String name = rs.getString("name");
                System.out.println(roll + " " + name + " " + rs.getString(3));
            }
        } catch (SQLException e) {                      // wrong SQL, missing table, constraint violation…
            System.out.println("DB error: " + e.getMessage());
        }
    }
}
  • ResultSet gettersgetInt, getString, getDouble, getDate, getBoolean, getObject — Java type must fit the SQL type
  • Column index starts at 1, not 0
  • ResultSetMetaData md = rs.getMetaData(); md.getColumnCount(); md.getColumnName(i) — inspect a result dynamically
  • Compile & runput driver jar on classpath: javac Demo.java · java -cp .;sqlite-jdbc.jar Demo (Linux/Mac: : instead of ;)
PreparedStatement & DML from JDBC

PreparedStatement = SQL with ? placeholders, compiled once and reused with different values. Faster and, above all, prevents SQL injection. Use it whenever values come from variables/users.

Query with a parameterint dno = 20;
String sql = "SELECT * FROM students WHERE deptno = ?";          // ? = parameter / placeholder
try (PreparedStatement ps = con.prepareStatement(sql)) {         // SQL given when preparing (not in executeQuery)
    ps.setInt(1, dno);                                            // parameter numbers start at 1
    ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery();                             // no argument here
    while (rs.next()) System.out.println(rs.getString("name"));
}
INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE → executeUpdate (NOT executeQuery)String ins = "INSERT INTO students (rollno, name, city, deptno) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)";
try (PreparedStatement ps = con.prepareStatement(ins)) {
    ps.setInt(1, 16);  ps.setString(2, "Smith");  ps.setString(3, "Delhi");  ps.setInt(4, 10);
    int rows = ps.executeUpdate();                               // returns number of rows affected
    System.out.println(rows + " row inserted");
} catch (SQLException e) {
    System.out.println(e.getMessage());                          // e.g. UNIQUE constraint failed: students.rollno (primary key)
}
MethodUse
executeQuery()SELECT → ResultSet
executeUpdate()INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE / DDL → int count
execute()any SQL → boolean (true if a ResultSet)
setInt / setString / setDouble / setDate / setNull(index, value)bind ? values
Never build SQL by string concatenation with user input ("… WHERE name='" + name + "'") — SQL injection (' OR '1'='1). Use ?.
Transactions — all-or-nothingcon.setAutoCommit(false);                       // default is true = every statement commits immediately
try {
    debit.executeUpdate();  credit.executeUpdate();
    con.commit();                                // make both permanent
} catch (SQLException e) {
    con.rollback();                              // undo both
}
CallableStatement — stored proceduresCallableStatement cs = con.prepareCall("{call raise_salary(?, ?)}");
cs.setInt(1, 101);  cs.setDouble(2, 10.0);  cs.execute();
Every enterprise app: login systems, e-commerce orders, inventory, banking transactions — Java code + SQL through JDBC (or JPA/Hibernate on top of it).
