AWT — GUI Components, Event Handling, Layouts & Animation

Lectures 29.1 – 29.2
GUI & AWT Basics
  • Interfacea point of communication between user and program. CUI/CLI (character interface, e.g. typing dir) vs GUI (windows, buttons, text boxes, mouse) — GUI is friendlier, event-driven
  • AWT (Abstract Window Toolkit)Java's original GUI library, package java.awt (+ java.awt.event). Components are heavyweight — each one uses a native peer of the operating system, so the look depends on the OS. (Swing and JavaFX came later)
  • VocabularyComponent (button, label…) · Container (holds components) · Layout manager · Event · Listener
Class hierarchy (important part)Object
 └─ Component  (Button, Label, TextField, TextArea, Checkbox, Choice, List, Scrollbar, Canvas, Container…)
     └─ Container
         ├─ Panel  (── Applet)
         └─ Window
             ├─ Frame   ← main application window (title bar, border, menu bar)
             └─ Dialog / FileDialog
ContainerNotesDefault layout
Frametop-level window, has title, borders, minimise/maximise/closeBorderLayout
Panelinvisible sub-area inside a frame to group componentsFlowLayout
Dialog, FileDialogpop-up windows (modal or not)BorderLayout
Building a Frame — Three Ways

Important Frame methods to remember: setTitle, setSize(w,h), setBounds(x,y,w,h), setVisible(true), setLayout(lm), add(component), setBackground(Color), setLocation, setResizable.

Approach 1 — create a Frame object in main (simplest)import java.awt.*;
public class FirstApp {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        Frame f = new Frame("My First App");
        f.setSize(400, 300);
        f.setLayout(new FlowLayout());
        f.add(new Label("Name:"));
        f.add(new TextField(20));
        f.add(new Button("OK"));
        f.setVisible(true);                            // frame is invisible until you say this!
    }
}
Approach 2 — write your own frame class; the application class HAS-A frame (containment)class MyFrame extends Frame {                          // IS-A Frame (inheritance)
    MyFrame() {
        super("My Frame");                             // super → Frame's constructor sets the title
        setSize(400, 300);  setLayout(new FlowLayout());
        add(new Button("Click"));
        setVisible(true);
    }
}
public class FirstApp { public static void main(String[] a) { new MyFrame(); } }   // "has-a": FirstApp has a frame object
Approach 3 — the application class itself extends Frame (most common)public class FirstApp extends Frame {
    Label l = new Label("Hello");  TextField t = new TextField(15);  Button b = new Button("Show");
    FirstApp() {                                       // components are created & added in the constructor
        super("Approach 3");
        setLayout(new FlowLayout());
        add(l); add(t); add(b);
        setSize(300, 200);
        setVisible(true);
    }
    public static void main(String[] args) { new FirstApp(); }
}
  • Is-a vs Has-a*is-a* = inheritance (extends Frame); *has-a* = containment (holding an object)
  • this in the constructorrefers to the object still being built; the frame is complete only once the constructor finishes — that is why you can't hand this out carelessly
  • Closingclicking ✕ does nothing in AWT until you handle WindowEvent (see Events)
AWT Components — Label, TextField, TextArea, Button
ComponentConstructors / key methodsNotes
Labelnew Label("text", Label.CENTER) · setText/getTextstatic text; alignment LEFT/CENTER/RIGHT
TextFieldnew TextField("init", 20) · getText/setText · setEchoChar('*') · setEditable(false)one line; password field with echo char
TextAreanew TextArea(rows, cols) · append(s) · getText · setText · getSelectedTextmany lines; scrollbars by default
Buttonnew Button("OK") · setLabel/getLabel · setEnabled(false)generates ActionEvent
Canvasoverride paint()blank area for drawing
TextField pass = new TextField(12);
pass.setEchoChar('*');                     // typed characters appear as ****
TextArea ta = new TextArea("", 5, 30);     // rows, columns
ta.append("line 1\n");
Button b = new Button("Login");
b.setEnabled(false);                       // disabled until something happens
add(pass); add(ta); add(b);
  • Data typesTextField.getText() always returns String → Integer.parseInt(t.getText()) for numbers
  • A TextField shows only 20 characters of width but holds more (you scroll) — width is set by columns
AWT Components — Checkbox, Radio (CheckboxGroup), Choice, List, Scrollbar
Checkbox & radio buttonsCheckbox c1 = new Checkbox("Java");                 // independent on/off
Checkbox c2 = new Checkbox("Python", true);         // initially selected
CheckboxGroup g = new CheckboxGroup();              // group makes them RADIO buttons – only ONE can be selected
Checkbox r1 = new Checkbox("Male", g, true);
Checkbox r2 = new Checkbox("Female", g, false);
System.out.println(c1.getState() + " " + g.getSelectedCheckbox().getLabel());
Choice (drop-down) and List (scrolling list)Choice ch = new Choice();
ch.add("Delhi"); ch.add("Mumbai"); ch.add("Indore");          // one item selectable
System.out.println(ch.getSelectedItem() + " " + ch.getSelectedIndex());

List lst = new List(4, false);                                // visible rows = 4, multipleMode = false → only ONE item may be selected
lst.add("C"); lst.add("C++"); lst.add("Java");
// new List(4, true) → multiple selection: Ctrl+click = random items, Shift+click = continuous range
String[] sel = lst.getSelectedItems();
ScrollbarScrollbar sb = new Scrollbar(Scrollbar.HORIZONTAL, 0, 10, 0, 110);   // orientation, initial value, visible amount, min, max
sb.addAdjustmentListener(e -> label.setText("Value: " + sb.getValue()));   // AdjustmentListener.adjustmentValueChanged
ComponentEventListener method
Button, TextField (Enter), MenuItem, List (double-click)ActionEventactionPerformed
Checkbox, Choice, ListItemEventitemStateChanged
TextField / TextArea (any change)TextEventtextValueChanged
ScrollbarAdjustmentEventadjustmentValueChanged
Old code used List.addItem(); the modern method is add(). Use null layout (setLayout(null) + setBounds) if you want to position components yourself — not recommended for resizing windows.
Event Handling — the Delegation Event Model

Event handling = making the GUI respond. Three things: ① Event source (component that generates an event when the user acts) · ② Event object (carries details) · ③ Listener (an object whose method is called). You register the listener with the source.

1

Pick the component & event

e.g. Button → ActionEvent

2

Write a listener

a class that implements the listener interface (ActionListener) and overrides all its methods (else the class must be abstract)

3

Register

button.addActionListener(listenerObject);

4

Handle

your method runs when the user acts (e.g. clicks) — runs on the GUI event thread

public class ClickApp extends Frame implements ActionListener {      // this class is the listener
    Button b = new Button("Click me");   Label l = new Label("            ");
    ClickApp() {
        super("Events");  setLayout(new FlowLayout());
        add(b); add(l);
        b.addActionListener(this);                                     // register 'this' as listener
        setSize(250, 150); setVisible(true);
    }
    @Override public void actionPerformed(ActionEvent e) {             // called on click
        l.setText("Button clicked!");
    }
    public static void main(String[] a) { new ClickApp(); }
}
Listener interfaceMethods (all must be overridden)Source
ActionListeneractionPerformed(ActionEvent)Button, TextField, MenuItem
ItemListeneritemStateChanged(ItemEvent)Checkbox, Choice, List
TextListenertextValueChanged(TextEvent)TextComponent
AdjustmentListeneradjustmentValueChanged(AdjustmentEvent)Scrollbar
KeyListenerkeyPressed, keyReleased, keyTyped (KeyEvent: getKeyChar(), getKeyCode())any component with focus
MouseListenermouseClicked, mousePressed, mouseReleased, mouseEntered, mouseExitedany component
MouseMotionListenermouseDragged, mouseMovedany component
WindowListenerwindowOpened, windowClosing, windowClosed, windowIconified (minimised), windowDeiconified (restored), windowActivated, windowDeactivatedWindow/Frame
FocusListenerfocusGained, focusLostany component
  • Event object methodse.getSource() (which component), e.getActionCommand() (button label), MouseEvent.getX()/getY()/getButton()/getClickCount() — mouse used to have left, right and middle button
  • Which component firedif (e.getSource() == b1) … else if (e.getSource() == b2) …
  • Closing the window: handle windowClosing and call System.exit(0) (0 = normal termination; a non-zero code tells the operating system an error occurred)
5 Ways to Write a Listener (Same Result)
1) The frame class implements the listener  (shown above)public class A extends Frame implements ActionListener { … b.addActionListener(this); … }
2) A separate / inner class as listener (inner class can use frame's components directly, only visible inside the frame)class B extends Frame {
    Button b = new Button("OK");  Label l = new Label();
    class Handler implements ActionListener {
        public void actionPerformed(ActionEvent e) { l.setText("clicked"); }
    }
    B() { b.addActionListener(new Handler()); }
}
3) Adapter class — override ONLY the method you need// A listener with many methods (WindowListener has 7) forces you to write ALL of them (empty bodies).
// Adapter classes (WindowAdapter, MouseAdapter, KeyAdapter, MouseMotionAdapter, FocusAdapter…)
// already give EMPTY bodies – you EXTEND the adapter and override just one:
addWindowListener(new WindowAdapter() {
    @Override public void windowClosing(WindowEvent e) { System.exit(0); }
});
class M extends MouseAdapter { public void mouseClicked(MouseEvent e) { System.out.println(e.getX() + "," + e.getY()); } }
4) Anonymous inner class (object created "on the fly", no reference / name)b.addActionListener(new ActionListener() {
    public void actionPerformed(ActionEvent e) { l.setText("anonymous"); }
});
5) Lambda expression — only for listeners with ONE abstract methodb.addActionListener(e -> l.setText("lambda"));                 // ActionListener has a single method
addWindowListener(new WindowAdapter() { public void windowClosing(WindowEvent e) { System.exit(0); } });  // WindowListener has 7 → use adapter
Listener interfaceAdapter class
Withimplements — must write all methodsextends — override only what you need
Exists forevery listeneronly listeners with more than one method (ActionListener has just one → no adapter, "why need an adapter for one method?")
Adapter is–abstract (so you can't instantiate it directly, though methods have empty bodies)
In an already-extending class✔ (class can implement many interfaces)✘ Java allows only one parent, so use an inner/anonymous class
Implement ActionListener and MouseListener in one class and it becomes abstract until you override every method of both. Lambdas work only where the interface has exactly one abstract method (it may have static/default methods).
Layout Managers

A layout manager positions components in a container automatically (setLayout(...)). If you don't want any → setLayout(null) + setBounds(x, y, w, h).

LayoutHow it arrangesDefault for
FlowLayoutleft→right in rows, wraps like text; new FlowLayout(FlowLayout.LEFT, hgap, vgap) — e.g. 100 pixels between componentsPanel, Applet
BorderLayout5 regions: NORTH, SOUTH, EAST, WEST, CENTER — one component per region (CENTER takes the rest); add(c, BorderLayout.NORTH)Frame, Dialog
GridLayoutequal cells in rows × columns — new GridLayout(3, 2) = 3 rows 2 columns; cannot skip a cell, fills row by row–
GridBagLayoutflexible grid like GridLayout but you may leave cells empty, components may span cells & have different sizes (GridBagConstraints: gridx, gridy, gridwidth, gridheight, weightx, fill, insets)–
CardLayoutcomponents/panels stacked like cards — only one visible; first(p) next(p) previous(p) last(p) show(p,"name")–
nullabsolute positions with setBounds–
Border + Grid + CardsetLayout(new BorderLayout(5, 5));                         // hgap, vgap
add(new Button("North"), BorderLayout.NORTH);
add(new TextArea(), BorderLayout.CENTER);

Panel grid = new Panel(new GridLayout(3, 2, 10, 10));      // 3 rows, 2 columns
for (int i = 1; i <= 6; i++) grid.add(new Button("B" + i));
add(grid, BorderLayout.SOUTH);                              // panels let you MIX layouts

CardLayout cl = new CardLayout();
Panel cards = new Panel(cl);
cards.add(new Panel(), "first");  cards.add(new Panel(), "second");
cl.show(cards, "second");     cl.next(cards);
  • Nested layoutsthe classic trick — a frame (BorderLayout) containing panels, each with its own layout
  • Panel → default FlowLayout · Frame → default BorderLayout — a component added without a region goes to CENTER, and only the last one is visible!
  • Calculator keypadGridLayout(4,4)
Menus, Dialogs & FileDialog
MenuBar mb = new MenuBar();
Menu file = new Menu("File");
MenuItem open = new MenuItem("Open"), exit = new MenuItem("Exit");
file.add(open); file.addSeparator(); file.add(exit);
mb.add(file);
setMenuBar(mb);                                        // frames only
exit.addActionListener(e -> System.exit(0));

FileDialog fd = new FileDialog(this, "Choose file", FileDialog.LOAD);
fd.setVisible(true);                                    // blocks until closed
String path = fd.getDirectory() + fd.getFile();

Dialog d = new Dialog(this, "Message", true);           // true = modal (blocks the parent)
d.add(new Label("Saved!")); d.setSize(150, 80); d.setVisible(true);
Drawing — paint(), Graphics & Colors

Override paint(Graphics g) to draw. Never call paint() yourself — call repaint(), which asks AWT to schedule update() → paint() at a proper time with a correct Graphics object.

public class DrawApp extends Frame {
    DrawApp() { setSize(400, 400); setVisible(true); }
    @Override public void paint(Graphics g) {               // called automatically when the window must be drawn
        g.setColor(Color.RED);
        g.drawLine(20, 50, 200, 50);
        g.drawRect(20, 70, 100, 60);       g.fillRect(140, 70, 100, 60);
        g.drawOval(20, 150, 100, 80);      g.fillOval(140, 150, 80, 80);       // x, y, width, height of bounding box
        g.setColor(new Color(0, 100, 200)); // RGB
        g.setFont(new Font("Arial", Font.BOLD, 20));
        g.drawString("Hello AWT", 50, 300);
    }
    public static void main(String[] a) { new DrawApp(); }
}
  • Coordinatesorigin (0,0) at top-left; x → right, y → down; units = pixels (450 height = 450 pixels). The title bar occupies the top ~30 px of a Frame
  • Why not call paint directly?you don't have a valid Graphics, and AWT calls it itself on resize/expose. repaint() = "please redraw"
  • Colorsconstants Color.RED … or new Color(r, g, b) 0–255
Animation with a Thread

A frame already extends Frame, so it can't also extend Thread → implement Runnable, create a Thread, and in run() change coordinates, repaint(), sleep().

Bouncing ballpublic class Ball extends Frame implements Runnable {
    int x = 20, dx = 5;
    Ball() { setSize(400, 200); setVisible(true);  new Thread(this).start(); }     // this = the Runnable
    public void run() {
        while (true) {
            x += dx;
            if (x > 350 || x < 10) dx = -dx;                  // bounce at walls
            repaint();                                        // paint() will draw the ball at the new x
            try { Thread.sleep(30); } catch (InterruptedException e) { }
        }
    }
    public void paint(Graphics g) { g.setColor(Color.BLUE); g.fillOval(x, 80, 30, 30); }
    public static void main(String[] a) { new Ball(); }
}
Clocks/marquees (thread + repaint), simple games, drawing pads (mouseDragged + drawLine), form applications.
Practice each component with its event: Button→ActionEvent, Checkbox→ItemEvent, Scrollbar→AdjustmentEvent, TextField→TextEvent, mouse & keyboard events on the frame.

Swing — Lightweight GUI Components

Lectures 30.1
Swing vs AWT

Assumes you know the AWT section (events, layouts). Swing is a newer, richer GUI toolkit — all its classes are part of JFC (Java Foundation Classes), package javax.swing.

AWTSwing
Componentsheavyweight (native OS peers)lightweight — drawn by Java itself (pure Java)
Look & feeldepends on the OSpluggable look-and-feel — default Metal; Nimbus, Windows, GTK, Motif
Class namesFrame, Button, TextFieldJ prefix: JFrame, JButton, JTextField (AWT components are a subset — every AWT component has a Swing version, plus many more)
Extrasfewtables, trees, tabbed panes, split panes, progress bars, sliders, spinners, internal frames, dialogs, file/colour choosers
Paintingoverride paint(Graphics)override paintComponent(Graphics)
Eventsjava.awt.eventsame plus javax.swing.event (ChangeListener, ListSelectionListener, TreeSelectionListener…)
Closing windownever closes by itselfsetDefaultCloseOperation(JFrame.EXIT_ON_CLOSE)
  • Importsjavax.swing.*; you still need java.awt.* (layouts, Color, Font) and java.awt.event.* (listeners) — *"cannot find symbol: FlowLayout"* means java.awt is not imported. Import only what you use
  • Some classes live elsewhereDateFormat is in java.text, not swing
  • Look & feelUIManager.setLookAndFeel(UIManager.getSystemLookAndFeelClassName());
A Swing Application — Skeleton
import javax.swing.*;
import java.awt.*;
import java.awt.event.*;

public class MyFrame extends JFrame implements ActionListener {
    JLabel lbl = new JLabel("Name:");
    JTextField txt = new JTextField(15);
    JButton btn = new JButton("Show");

    MyFrame() {
        super("Swing Demo");
        setDefaultCloseOperation(JFrame.EXIT_ON_CLOSE);       // what to do when the frame is closed
        setLayout(new FlowLayout());                           // components go to the CONTENT PANE (default BorderLayout)
        add(lbl); add(txt); add(btn);
        btn.addActionListener(this);
        setSize(350, 150);                                     // no size → nothing visible!
        setLocationRelativeTo(null);                           // centre on screen
        setVisible(true);
    }
    public void actionPerformed(ActionEvent e) { JOptionPane.showMessageDialog(this, "Hello " + txt.getText()); }

    public static void main(String[] args) {
        SwingUtilities.invokeLater(() -> new MyFrame());        // build GUI on the Event Dispatch Thread (recommended)
    }
}
setDefaultCloseOperation(…)Effect
JFrame.EXIT_ON_CLOSEends the program (replaces AWT's windowClosing + System.exit)
JFrame.HIDE_ON_CLOSEhides (default)
JFrame.DISPOSE_ON_CLOSEdisposes this window; program ends after the last one
JFrame.DO_NOTHING_ON_CLOSEignore (handle WindowEvent yourself)
  • JFrame's content panegetContentPane().add(c) — add(c) works directly since Java 5. Default layout BorderLayout; JPanel default FlowLayout
  • Stop button in the IDEred = the program was stopped forcibly (an AWT frame without close handling)
Text Components — JTextField, JPasswordField, JTextArea, JFormattedTextField
JTextField name = new JTextField("initial", 20);
JPasswordField pw = new JPasswordField(12);                    // masked input
char[] secret = pw.getPassword();                              // char[] not String (can be wiped)
pw.setEchoChar('#');

JTextArea ta = new JTextArea(6, 30);
ta.setLineWrap(true);  ta.setWrapStyleWord(true);
JScrollPane sp = new JScrollPane(ta);                          // Swing does NOT give scrollbars automatically → wrap in JScrollPane
add(sp);
JFormattedTextField — restricts input to a format (date, number, currency)DateFormat df = new SimpleDateFormat("dd/MM/yyyy");                   // java.text package
JFormattedTextField date = new JFormattedTextField(df);
date.setColumns(10);                                                   // give size or it's invisible
date.setValue(new java.util.Date(0));                                  // give an initial value (0 = start of epoch)

NumberFormat cf = NumberFormat.getCurrencyInstance(Locale.US);         // $1,234.50
JFormattedTextField money = new JFormattedTextField(cf);
money.setValue(0.0);                                                   // without an initial value nothing can be typed!
money.setColumns(10);
double v = ((Number) money.getValue()).doubleValue();

MaskFormatter phone = new MaskFormatter("(###) ###-####");             // # = digit
JFormattedTextField ph = new JFormattedTextField(phone);
  • Similar to AWTgetText/setText/setEditable/setEnabled, key events (KeyListener) work the same
  • Formatted fielduse getValue() (typed object: Date/Number) rather than getText(); call commitEdit() if needed
  • setColumns() / preferred size must be given or the field can appear empty
Buttons — JButton, JCheckBox, JRadioButton, ButtonGroup
JButton b = new JButton("Save", new ImageIcon("save.png"));      // text + icon
b.setToolTipText("Save the file");       // feedback text on hover
b.setMnemonic('S');                       // Alt+S
b.setActionCommand("SAVE");               // identifier that can differ from the visible label
// in actionPerformed:  if (e.getActionCommand().equals("SAVE")) …

JCheckBox c1 = new JCheckBox("Java", true);          // isSelected()
JRadioButton r1 = new JRadioButton("Male", true);
JRadioButton r2 = new JRadioButton("Female");
ButtonGroup bg = new ButtonGroup();                   // ONLY ONE selected at a time
bg.add(r1); bg.add(r2);                               // group is logical – add BOTH to the frame separately as well
add(r1); add(r2);
r1.addItemListener(e -> System.out.println(r1.isSelected()));
  • ButtonGroup is not a component (not shown) — it only enforces single selection
  • EventsActionEvent (click) or ItemEvent (state change)
  • Action command freedomthe label shown and the command string can be different (handy for icons-only buttons)
Lists & Selections — JComboBox, JList, JSpinner, JSlider, JProgressBar
String[] countries = {"India", "USA", "UK"};
JComboBox<String> cb = new JComboBox<>(countries);       // drop-down; ONE item visible & selectable
cb.addItem("Japan");   String sel = (String) cb.getSelectedItem();   cb.setEditable(true);

String[] months = {"Jan", "Feb", "Mar", "Apr", "May", "Jun"};
JList<String> list = new JList<>(months);                // several items visible; multiple selection possible
list.setVisibleRowCount(5);                              // only 5 items visible at a time
list.setSelectionMode(ListSelectionModel.MULTIPLE_INTERVAL_SELECTION);
add(new JScrollPane(list));                              // scrollbar needs a scroll pane
List<String> chosen = list.getSelectedValuesList();

JSpinner sp1 = new JSpinner(new SpinnerNumberModel(10, 0, 100, 5));      // value, min, max, step
JSpinner dateSp = new JSpinner(new SpinnerDateModel());
sp1.addChangeListener(e -> field.setText(sp1.getValue().toString()));    // javax.swing.event.ChangeListener

JSlider s = new JSlider(JSlider.HORIZONTAL, 0, 100, 50);   // default orientation is horizontal; min, max, initial
s.setMajorTickSpacing(10); s.setMinorTickSpacing(5);       // ticks: 0 10 20 30 …
s.setPaintTicks(true); s.setPaintLabels(true);
s.addChangeListener(e -> bar.setValue(s.getValue()));

JProgressBar bar = new JProgressBar(0, 100);
bar.setStringPainted(true);                    // show percentage text
bar.setIndeterminate(true);                    // unknown duration (moving animation)
ComboBoxJList
Selectionsinglesingle or multiple
Visibleone item (drop-down)many at once (setVisibleRowCount)
Scrolling–wrap in JScrollPane
EventActionListener/ItemListenerListSelectionListener (javax.swing.event)
  • Layout in an exampleframe BorderLayout → slider NORTH, panel CENTER, progress bar SOUTH (progress bar inside a panel appears at its natural size)
Container Components — JScrollPane, JTabbedPane, JSplitPane, JTree
JTabbedPane tabs = new JTabbedPane();                     // AWT used CardLayout for this purpose
tabs.addTab("Personal", new JPanel());
tabs.addTab("Address",  new JPanel());

JSplitPane split = new JSplitPane(JSplitPane.HORIZONTAL_SPLIT, leftComp, rightComp);   // constants belong to JSplitPane
split.setDividerLocation(150);   split.setOneTouchExpandable(true);

// JTree: build a hierarchy of DefaultMutableTreeNode (a tree cannot be built from an interface – use the class)
DefaultMutableTreeNode root = new DefaultMutableTreeNode("C:/myjava");
for (String s : new String[]{"src", "bin", "lib"}) root.add(new DefaultMutableTreeNode(s));
JTree tree = new JTree(root);                              // root is used to create the tree
add(new JScrollPane(tree));                                // scroll pane given at creation
tree.addTreeSelectionListener(e -> label.setText(e.getPath().getLastPathComponent().toString()));
  • JTreeevery node = DefaultMutableTreeNode; expand/collapse built in; file-explorer style demos show folders as nodes (File.listFiles() recursion builds the tree)
  • Scroll panewhen constructing new JScrollPane(component) you give the component to scroll
  • Split paneconstants HORIZONTAL_SPLIT / VERTICAL_SPLIT are defined inside JSplitPane
Menus, Dialogs, Choosers & Internal Frames
Menu barJMenuBar mb = new JMenuBar();

Ready-made dialogsJOptionPane.showMessageDialog(this, "Saved!", "Info", JOptionPane.INFORMATION_MESSAGE);

if (r == JOptionPane.YES_OPTION) System.exit(0);
String name = JOptionPane.showInputDialog(this, "Enter name:");

JFileChooser fc = new JFileChooser();                     // Open = load, Save = save
if (fc.showOpenDialog(this) == JFileChooser.APPROVE_OPTION) System.out.println(fc.getSelectedFile());
if (fc.showSaveDialog(this) == JFileChooser.APPROVE_OPTION) { /* write to fc.getSelectedFile() */ }
Color c = JColorChooser.showDialog(this, "Pick colour", Color.YELLOW);   // default selected yellow
panel.setBackground(c);
Multiple-document interface — JDesktopPane + JInternalFrameJDesktopPane desk = new JDesktopPane();                   // a desktop that hosts internal frames
setContentPane(desk);
static int counter = 0;                                   // one counter kept for the CLASS (static) → "Document 1, 2, 3…"
void newDoc() {
    JInternalFrame f = new JInternalFrame("Document " + (++counter), true, true, true, true);   // resizable, closable, maximizable, iconifiable
    f.add(new JScrollPane(new JTextArea()));               // default BorderLayout → scroll pane fills it
    f.setBounds(30, 30, 300, 200);                         // always open at the same location
    f.setVisible(true);                                    // visible = true is NOT default for internal frames
    desk.add(f);
}
  • Menu itemsone ActionListener for all → e.getActionCommand() or e.getSource() decides (Open, Save, Exit)
  • Modal dialogsJOptionPane blocks until the user answers and returns the option chosen
Drawing in Swing — paintComponent
class DrawPanel extends JPanel {                          // draw on a JPanel, not directly on the frame
    @Override protected void paintComponent(Graphics g) { // Swing method name (AWT used paint)
        super.paintComponent(g);                          // clear background first
        g.setColor(Color.MAGENTA);
        g.fillOval(50, 50, 100, 100);
        g.drawString("Swing drawing", 60, 180);
    }
}
add(new DrawPanel());                                     // call repaint() to redraw
  • Ruleoverride paintComponent in a JPanel/JComponent; never call it directly — use repaint()
  • Animationjavax.swing.Timer(delay, e -> { x++; repaint(); }).start(); (runs on the EDT — safer than a raw Thread)
Timer t = new Timer(30, e -> { x += 3; panel.repaint(); });
t.start();
Layout managers from AWT (Flow, Border, Grid, GridBag, Card) work unchanged in Swing; Swing adds BoxLayout (new BoxLayout(panel, BoxLayout.Y_AXIS)) and GroupLayout.
31

JavaFX — Modern GUI (Stage, Scene, Controls, Layouts, Pickers)

Lectures 31.1 – 31.6
JavaFX — Idea & Application Structure

After AWT and Swing, JavaFX is the modern GUI framework (packages javafx.application, javafx.stage, javafx.scene.*). Writing functionality and designing the UI are different tasks: JavaFX lets you separate them with FXML (XML-based UI markup, editable in Scene Builder) — designer and programmer work independently. (Setup/installation is not covered here.)

ConceptMeaning
Applicationabstract class — your main class extends Application; it has one abstract method: start(Stage) which you must override
launch(args)called from main() — starts the JavaFX runtime, which creates the Stage and calls start(); main is only the launcher
Stagethe window (primary stage is supplied to start)
Scenethe content shown inside a stage (one scene at a time; you can swap scenes — different scene, same stage)
Scene graphtree of nodes: root (a *Parent* layout) → child controls → leaf controls
Nodebase class — almost every visual class inherits from Node (controls, layouts, shapes, images)
init() / stop()optional lifecycle: before start / when the application closes
Always the same structureStage  ──►  Scene  ──►  Parent (layout: HBox, VBox, BorderPane…)  ──►  child controls / leaf controls (Button, Label…)
Minimal applicationimport javafx.application.Application;
import javafx.scene.Scene;
import javafx.scene.control.Button;
import javafx.scene.layout.StackPane;
import javafx.stage.Stage;

public class HelloFX extends Application {
    @Override
    public void start(Stage stage) {                  // the ONLY abstract method
        Button b = new Button("Click me");            // leaf control
        StackPane root = new StackPane(b);            // parent layout
        Scene scene = new Scene(root, 300, 200);      // scene = root + size
        stage.setTitle("First JavaFX");
        stage.setScene(scene);                        // put the scene on the stage
        stage.show();                                 // display
    }
    public static void main(String[] args) { launch(args); }     // launches the application
}
  • Adding childrenroot.getChildren().add(node) / addAll(a, b, c) (or pass them to the layout constructor)
  • Error *"HelloFX is not abstract and does not override abstract method start(Stage)"* means you forgot start
  • Compared to Swingno JFrame; Stage/Scene separation; CSS styling; FXML; properties & bindings
Common Properties & Methods of Controls
MemberPurpose
setText(s) / getText()caption / content (Label, Button, TextField…)
setPrefSize(w,h) setMinSize setMaxSize setPrefWidthsizes
setDisable(true)disabled
setVisible(false)hidden
setOpacity(0.5)transparency
setFont(Font.font("Arial", FontWeight.BOLD, 18))font (weight → FontWeight import)
setTextFill(Color.RED)text colour
setWrapText(true)long text goes onto multiple lines
setStyle("-fx-background-color: yellow; -fx-font-size: 16px;")inline CSS
setTooltip(new Tooltip("Hint"))hover text
setId("save")id for CSS/lookup
setLayoutX / setLayoutY / relocate(x,y)manual position (unmanaged layouts)
Button.setDefaultButton(true)pressed by Enter key
Button.setCancelButton(true)pressed by Esc key
Button b = new Button("A very long caption that needs wrapping");
b.setPrefWidth(120);  b.setWrapText(true);
b.setDefaultButton(true);
b.setFont(Font.font("Verdana", FontWeight.BOLD, 14));
b.setStyle("-fx-background-color: #2e86de; -fx-text-fill: white;");
Event Handling in JavaFX

Register a handler with setOnXxx; the handler implements EventHandler<T> whose single method is handle(T event).

Way 1 — the class implements EventHandler<ActionEvent>public class App extends Application implements EventHandler<ActionEvent> {   // ActionEvent → javafx.event.ActionEvent (not AWT!)
    Button b1 = new Button("One"), b2 = new Button("Two");   Label l = new Label();
    @Override public void start(Stage s) {
        b1.setOnAction(this);   b2.setOnAction(this);              // this = handler object
        s.setScene(new Scene(new VBox(10, b1, b2, l), 250, 150));  s.show();
    }
    @Override public void handle(ActionEvent e) {                   // MUST override handle
        if (e.getSource() == b1) l.setText("One clicked"); else l.setText("Two clicked");
    }
    public static void main(String[] a) { launch(a); }
}
Way 2 — anonymous class · Way 3 — lambda (interface has one method)b1.setOnAction(new EventHandler<ActionEvent>() {
    public void handle(ActionEvent e) { l.setText("anon"); }
});
b1.setOnAction(e -> l.setText("lambda"));
b2.setOnMouseClicked(e -> System.out.println(e.getX() + "," + e.getY()));
tf.setOnKeyReleased(e -> l.setText(tf.getText()));
  • Event typesActionEvent, MouseEvent, KeyEvent, ScrollEvent, WindowEvent; handlers: setOnAction, setOnMouseClicked, setOnMouseEntered, setOnKeyPressed, setOnDragDetected…
  • Import pitfallchoose javafx.event.ActionEvent — java.awt.event.ActionEvent won't work
  • Change listeners (properties)slider.valueProperty().addListener((obs, oldV, newV) -> …); bind: label.textProperty().bind(tf.textProperty())
Basic Controls — Label, TextField, RadioButton, CheckBox, ChoiceBox
Label lbl = new Label("Name:");
TextField tf = new TextField();  tf.setPromptText("Enter name");  tf.setPrefColumnCount(15);
PasswordField pf = new PasswordField();
TextArea ta = new TextArea();  ta.setPrefRowCount(4);  ta.setWrapText(true);

RadioButton rb1 = new RadioButton("Male"), rb2 = new RadioButton("Female");

rb1.setToggleGroup(tg);  rb2.setToggleGroup(tg);  rb1.setSelected(true);
RadioButton chosen = (RadioButton) tg.getSelectedToggle();

CheckBox c1 = new CheckBox("Java"), c2 = new CheckBox("SQL");

HBox row2 = new HBox(10, rb1, rb2);                    // HBox 2: radio buttons
HBox row3 = new HBox(10, c1, c2);                      // HBox 3: check boxes
VBox root = new VBox(15, row1, row2, row3, cb);        // stack rows vertically
  • Controls fire ActionEvent when clicked/selected; getValue()/isSelected() read the state
  • Text weightFont.font(family, FontWeight.BOLD, size); big fonts need bigger preferred width
Pickers — DatePicker, ColorPicker, FileChooser
DatePicker dp = new DatePicker(LocalDate.now());          // uses java.time.LocalDate
dp.setOnAction(e -> {
    LocalDate d = dp.getValue();                          // selected date (LocalDate)
    label.setText(d.format(DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("dd MMM yyyy")));
});

ColorPicker cp = new ColorPicker(Color.WHITE);
cp.setOnAction(e -> {
    Color c = cp.getValue();
    String hex = c.toString().substring(2, 8);            // "0xff0000ff" → strip "0x", keep 6 hex letters "ff0000"
    textArea.setStyle("-fx-control-inner-background: #" + hex + ";");   // change TextArea colour by CSS
});

FileChooser fc = new FileChooser();
fc.setTitle("Open text file");
fc.getExtensionFilters().add(new FileChooser.ExtensionFilter("Text files", "*.txt"));
File f = fc.showOpenDialog(stage);                        // returns null if the user cancels
if (f != null) {
    try (BufferedReader br = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(f))) {
        textArea.clear();
        String line; while ((line = br.readLine()) != null) textArea.appendText(line + "\n");
    } catch (IOException ex) { new Alert(Alert.AlertType.ERROR, "Cannot open file").show(); }   // file missing / unreadable
}
File out = fc.showSaveDialog(stage);                      // save dialog
  • Replace, don't appendwhen showing a picked date remove the previous text first — *don't blindly insert a new date*
  • Color.toString() returns 0xRRGGBBAA — take 6 hex letters after 0x
  • AlertINFORMATION, WARNING, ERROR, CONFIRMATION; alert.showAndWait() returns the button pressed
  • Other pickers/controlsSlider, ProgressBar, Spinner, TableView, TreeView, WebView, MenuBar
ComboBox & ListView — Complete Form Application

Demonstrates ComboBox (drop-down, can be editable), ListView and a full customer-details form that stores data in a file and reloads it at start-up.

ComboBox<String> combo = new ComboBox<>();
combo.getItems().addAll("India", "USA", "UK");
combo.setPromptText("Select country");   combo.setEditable(true);
String c = combo.getValue();
combo.setOnAction(e -> label.setText(combo.getValue()));

ListView<String> lv = new ListView<>(FXCollections.observableArrayList("Jan", "Feb", "Mar"));
lv.setPrefHeight(80);
lv.getSelectionModel().setSelectionMode(SelectionMode.MULTIPLE);
String one = lv.getSelectionModel().getSelectedItem();
ObservableList<String> many = lv.getSelectionModel().getSelectedItems();
Customer form app: fields for id (auto-generated, read-only), name, address, city (ComboBox); buttons New / Save / Search. On start-up read everything from a file into a HashMap<Integer, Customer>; New = generate the next customer id and clear fields; Save = put into the map and write the map to the file; the id list appears in a ListView/ComboBox so clicking an id shows that customer's details.
class Customer implements Serializable {                    // details kept in one class
    int id; String name, address, city;
    Customer(int id, String n, String a, String c) { this.id = id; name = n; address = a; city = c; }
}
public class CustomerApp extends Application {
    HashMap<Integer, Customer> map = new HashMap<>();
    int nextId = 1;
    TextField tId = new TextField(), tName = new TextField(), tAddr = new TextField();
    ComboBox<String> cCity = new ComboBox<>();
    ListView<Integer> ids = new ListView<>();

    @Override public void start(Stage stage) {
        try {                                               // catch exceptions inside start()
            File f = new File("cust.dat");
            if (f.exists()) try (ObjectInputStream in = new ObjectInputStream(new FileInputStream(f))) {
                map = (HashMap<Integer, Customer>) in.readObject();    // reload at start-up
                nextId = map.size() + 1;
            }
        } catch (Exception e) { new Alert(Alert.AlertType.ERROR, e.toString()).show(); }
        ids.getItems().addAll(map.keySet());

        tId.setEditable(false);   tId.setText("" + nextId);
        cCity.getItems().addAll("Delhi", "Mumbai", "Indore");
        Button bNew = new Button("New"), bSave = new Button("Save");
        bNew.setOnAction(e -> { tId.setText("" + (++nextId)); tName.clear(); tAddr.clear(); });     // new id automatically
        bSave.setOnAction(e -> {
            int id = Integer.parseInt(tId.getText());
            map.put(id, new Customer(id, tName.getText(), tAddr.getText(), cCity.getValue()));
            if (!ids.getItems().contains(id)) ids.getItems().add(id);
            try (ObjectOutputStream out = new ObjectOutputStream(new FileOutputStream("cust.dat"))) { out.writeObject(map); }
            catch (IOException ex) { new Alert(Alert.AlertType.ERROR, ex.getMessage()).show(); }
        });
        ids.getSelectionModel().selectedItemProperty().addListener((o, ov, nv) -> {   // click id → show customer
            if (nv == null) return;
            Customer c = map.get(nv);  tId.setText("" + c.id); tName.setText(c.name); tAddr.setText(c.address); cCity.setValue(c.city);
        });

        GridPane form = new GridPane();  form.setHgap(10);  form.setVgap(8);  form.setPadding(new Insets(15));
        form.addRow(0, new Label("ID"), tId);   form.addRow(1, new Label("Name"), tName);
        form.addRow(2, new Label("Address"), tAddr);   form.addRow(3, new Label("City"), cCity);
        form.add(new HBox(10, bNew, bSave), 1, 4);
        stage.setScene(new Scene(new HBox(15, ids, form), 500, 300));
        stage.setTitle("Customers");   stage.show();
    }
    public static void main(String[] a) { launch(a); }
}
Set sizes on text fields (setPrefColumnCount) and alignment on containers (setAlignment(Pos.CENTER_LEFT)) so the form looks tidy.
Layouts & Panes
PaneBehaviourKey API
HBoxchildren in a horizontal rownew HBox(spacing, nodes…) · setAlignment(Pos.CENTER) · setPadding(new Insets(10)) · HBox.setHgrow(node, Priority.ALWAYS)
VBoxvertical columnnew VBox(10, …) · setSpacing
BorderPane5 regions top, bottom, left, right, centersetTop(n) setBottom setLeft setRight setCenter. Nothing specified → goes to center (only the last node added there stays visible); regions may be left empty; gap between regions by default 0 (use margins/insets)
FlowPanewraps like text (like AWT FlowLayout)new FlowPane(hgap, vgap) · horizontal or vertical orientation
GridPanerows & columns grid; cells may be spannedadd(node, columnIndex, rowIndex) · add(node, col, row, colSpan, rowSpan) · addRow(row, nodes…) — an empty row/column has size 0, so a missing third row makes the fourth appear directly below the second
StackPanechildren stacked on top of each other (centre)overlays: image + text, watermark
TilePaneequal-sized tilessetPrefColumns(3)
AnchorPanepin nodes to edgesAnchorPane.setTopAnchor(n, 10.0)
ScrollPanescroll large contentnew ScrollPane(node)
Border pane + imageBorderPane root = new BorderPane();
root.setTop(new Label("Header"));
root.setLeft(new Button("Left"));
root.setRight(new Button("Right"));
root.setBottom(new Label("Footer"));
root.setCenter(new TextArea());

Image img = new Image(new FileInputStream("photo.jpg"));      // Image ← FileInputStream(path)   (needs try/catch or throws)
ImageView iv = new ImageView(img);                             // ImageView ← Image
iv.setFitWidth(200);  iv.setPreserveRatio(true);               // "can't see the entire image" → resize with fitWidth/fitHeight
root.setCenter(iv);

GridPane g = new GridPane();
g.add(new Label("Row0 Col0"), 0, 0);  g.add(new Button("R0C1"), 1, 0);  g.add(new Button("Span"), 0, 1, 2, 1);
  • Steps to remembercreate controls → put them in layouts → put the top layout in a Scene → set the Scene on the Stage → show()
  • Nestingthe standard pattern — BorderPane with VBox/HBox/GridPane inside its regions
  • FittingPos.CENTER, Insets, Priority.ALWAYS, and setMaxWidth(Double.MAX_VALUE) make controls fill space
Business forms, dashboards, media viewers, desktop tools — with CSS themes and FXML designs.

Appendix — Golden Rules & Common Compile / Runtime Errors

Lectures all sections
Common Errors & What They Mean
MessageUsual causeFix
cannot find symbolmissing import, typo in name, variable out of scopeimport the class, check spelling / case
incompatible types: possible lossy conversion from double to intnarrowing without cast (int x = 5.5;)cast (int) or use double
incompatible types: int cannot be converted to booleanif (a = 5)use ==
variable might not have been initializedlocal variable used before assignmentgive it a value
missing return statementa path in a non-void method has no returnreturn on every path
class X is public, should be declared in a file named X.javafile name ≠ public class namerename file or class
non-static method … cannot be referenced from a static contextcalling instance method/variable from main directlycreate an object
unreported exception … must be caught or declared to be thrownchecked exception not handledtry-catch or throws
exception X has already been caughtcatch order wrongsubclass catch first
X is abstract; cannot be instantiatednew on abstract class/interfaceinstantiate a concrete subclass
… is not abstract and does not override abstract method …missing method implementationimplement all abstract methods
attempting to assign weaker access privilegesoverride reduces visibilitymake it public
cannot assign a value to final variablechanging a finalremove final or don't reassign
constructor X in class X cannot be applied to given typesno matching constructor (default one vanished)add the constructor / pass right arguments
x has private access in Xaccessing private member outside classuse getter/setter
unreachable statementcode after return/break/throw/infinite loopremove/reorder
Main method not found / NoSuchMethodError: mainsignature isn't public static void main(String[])fix signature
ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsExceptionindex < 0 or ≥ lengthloop < a.length
NullPointerExceptionusing a null reference (array of objects not filled, uninitialised object)create the object first
NumberFormatException / InputMismatchExceptiontext isn't a numbervalidate (regex) or catch
ClassCastExceptioninvalid downcast / raw collectionuse generics, instanceof first
ConcurrentModificationExceptionmodifying a collection while iteratingIterator.remove() / removeIf
StackOverflowErrorrecursion without base caseadd a base case
IllegalThreadStateExceptionstart() called twicecreate a new Thread
IllegalMonitorStateExceptionwait/notify outside synchronizedput inside synchronized
InvalidClassExceptionserialVersionUID mismatchdeclare serialVersionUID
Golden Rules Recap
  • Java basicsmain = public static void main(String[] args) · file name = public class name · javac compiles to byte code, java runs it on the JVM
  • Dataint/long/… sizes are fixed · decimals are double by default (5.5f for float) · byte+byte = int · char is 2 bytes unsigned
  • Stringsimmutable · compare with equals(), never == · substring end index is exclusive
  • Controlif (x == y) not = · break in every case · loops: while (unknown count), do-while (at least once), for (known count)
  • Arraysindex from 0 · length field (no brackets) · arrays are objects (heap) · for-each cannot modify
  • Methodspass-by-value only · overloading = different parameters (compile-time) · overriding = same signature (run-time)
  • OOPfields private, methods public · constructor = class name, no return type · super()/this() first line · a class extends one class, implements many interfaces
  • Abstract vs interfaceabstract class may hold state and code; interface = contract (default methods allowed)
  • staticshared by the class, no this · final :: value/method/class can't change/override/extend · abstract ⊥ final/static/private
  • Exceptionschecked (compiler forces) vs unchecked (runtime) · finally always runs · use try-with-resources for streams/connections
  • Threadsstart() not run() · shared data → synchronized · wait/notify inside synchronized
  • Collectionsinterface on the left · override equals + hashCode for hash-based keys · Comparable for natural order, Comparator for custom
  • Genericsno primitives (Integer not int) · ? extends read, ? super write
  • I/Obytes → InputStream/OutputStream, text → Reader/Writer · always close · serialization needs Serializable
  • JDBCuse PreparedStatement; executeQuery for SELECT, executeUpdate for DML

@end

I1

Java Today — the Honest Numbers (September 2026)

Lectures facts checked 24 Sep 2026
Where Java Stands Right Now

Java is not the hype language of 2026 — it is the default choice for large, long-lived business systems. Both statements are true, and the numbers show it.

MeasureValueWhat it tells you
TIOBE Index (Sep 2026)#4, rating 7.54 % (Python #1 at 17.76 %, C++ #3 at 8.67 %)search-popularity ranking; Java's share has slipped from 26.49 % in 2001
Stack Overflow Developer Survey 2025used by about 29–30 % of professional developersstill one of the most-used languages (down slightly from 30.6 % a year earlier)
Banking & finance developers (SO 2025, one analyst's count)roughly 44 % use Javafinance is Java's strongest sector
Current LTSJava 25 (released 16 Sep 2025)Oracle updates to Sep 2030 (paid extended support to 2033; free-use terms for Oracle JDK 25 updates run to Sep 2028); other vendors longer (e.g. Amazon Corretto to Jul 2032)
Newest releaseJava 27, released 15 Sep 2026 (non-LTS; supported until Mar 2027)six-month cadence since 2017
Next LTSJava 29 — planned Sep 2027LTS every two years
What companies actually run (JetBrains 2025)Java 21: 40 % · Java 17: 39 % · Java 8: 31 % (multi-select)enterprises upgrade slowly — old code keeps working
  • How to read the rank dropTIOBE counts *search/tutorial activity*, so AI-driven Python hype moves it a lot. It does not measure how many production systems or jobs exist. Java's *installed base* in enterprises is far larger than its buzz
  • Why the interview answer must be honestdon't say "Java is #1". Say "Java is one of the top languages by usage and the backbone of enterprise backends"
Rankings and survey figures change monthly/yearly — treat every number here as a September 2026 snapshot and re-check the source before quoting it in an interview or report.
The Java Release Model Companies Rely On
VersionTypeWhy it matters
Java 8 (2014)LTSlambdas, streams; still run by ~31 % of Java teams — huge legacy base
Java 11 (2018)LTSfirst widely-adopted "modern" baseline after the 6-month cadence began
Java 17 (Sep 2021)LTSrecords, sealed classes, text blocks; Spring Boot 3 requires 17+; Oracle premier support ends Sep 2026
Java 21 (Sep 2023)LTSvirtual threads, generational ZGC, pattern matching for switch, record patterns
Java 25 (Sep 2025)current LTSthe target for companies upgrading in 2026
Java 26 (Mar 2026) / 27 (Sep 2026)feature releasesfor early adopters; production teams wait for the next LTS (29)
  • Predictability is a featurea new JDK every March & September, LTS every two years — a CTO can *plan* upgrades years ahead
  • Backward compatibilityold .class files and source keep working; deprecated APIs live for many releases with warnings (you saw @Deprecated and the deprecated Date methods in the course)
  • Many vendors, one standard (OpenJDK)Oracle, Eclipse Temurin, Amazon Corretto, Azul, Microsoft, Red Hat, BellSoft… — companies can switch vendors without changing code, so they are not locked in
A bank on Java 17 today can move to 21 or 25 in stages (Netflix describes 17 → 21 as much easier than 8 → 17) instead of rewriting anything.
I2

The Reasons — Why Big Product Companies Choose Java

Lectures each reason linked to your course
Reason 1 — Write Once, Run Anywhere → Deploy Anywhere

Course link: Section 4 (Compiler + Interpreter, byte code, JVM).

  • What it means in a companybuild one JAR/WAR once in CI → run the same artifact on a developer laptop (Windows/Mac), a Linux server, a Docker container, Kubernetes, AWS/Azure/GCP. No per-OS rebuild
  • Why it matters at scalehundreds of services × thousands of servers — a *portable artifact* removes a whole class of "works on my machine" problems
  • Cloud-neutralevery big cloud ships Java runtimes and SDKs (AWS has its own OpenJDK build, Corretto)
  • Fixed data sizesint is always 4 bytes on every machine → identical behaviour everywhere (Section 2)
Reason 2 — Stability & Backward Compatibility Protect Billions in Business Logic
  • Old code keeps runningsource and bytecode written years ago compile/run on new JDKs — companies don't have to rewrite core banking, billing or order systems
  • Cost of a rewritea rewrite risks new bugs in money-handling code and costs years; upgrading Java versions is cheaper and incremental
  • Real signalNetflix kept much of its code on JDK 8 for a long time because of an in-house framework, then ran a company-wide migration to Spring Boot and newer JDKs (17 → 21) with automated tooling — Java *let them modernise in place*
  • 31 % of Java developers still use Java 8 (JetBrains 2025) — a sign that "if it works, keep it" is affordable on the JVM
Stability is why Java is strong in banks, insurance, telecom, government — domains where downtime or a wrong calculation is very costly.
Reason 3 — Performance at Scale: JIT + Garbage Collection

Course link: Section 4.5 (Execution Engine: interpreter, JIT compiler, Garbage Collector), Section 16 (heap/stack).

  • JIT (Just-In-Time)the JVM profiles running code and compiles the hot parts to optimised machine code — long-running servers get *faster* as they warm up, often approaching native speed
  • Automatic memory managementno malloc/free bugs, no dangling pointers; programmers focus on business logic
  • Modern collectorsG1 (default), ZGC / generational ZGC (very short pause times) — important for latency-sensitive services. Netflix's engineers describe adopting Java 21's generational ZGC across their microservice fleet to cut pause-related latency spikes
  • Tuning knobs & profilingJFR (Flight Recorder), heap dumps, jcmd, VisualVM — teams can *measure and fix* performance in production
Why long-running is a strengthstart ──► interpreter (slow, profiling) ──► hot methods → JIT compiled → optimised machine code ──► steady high throughput
Reason 4 — Concurrency Built Into the Platform

Course link: Section 19 (Threads, synchronized, wait/notify), Section 18/23 (streams).

  • Threads, monitors, synchronized, java.util.concurrent (executors, concurrent collections, locks, atomics) are part of the language & library since Java 1.0 / 5
  • Virtual threads (Java 21, JEP 444)lightweight JVM-scheduled threads → the simple thread-per-request style scales to huge numbers of concurrent requests without callback/reactive code. Netflix adopted them in Spring Boot services — and published a case where virtual threads got *pinned* on locks and caused hangs, then how they fixed it: proof that a real, well-supported platform lets big companies adopt features carefully
  • Threads + Collections + I/O togetherservers, queues and batch jobs are natural in Java (course sections 19, 23, 25, 27)
Read the Netflix post as a lesson: new features (virtual threads) can still have pitfalls. Big companies test heavily before rolling out.
Reason 5 — Strong Typing, OOP & Compile-Time Checking Suit Big Teams

Course link: Sections 11–17 (classes, inheritance, abstract, interface, packages, access modifiers), Section 24 (generics).

  • Compiler catches mistakes early — type errors, missing methods, unhandled checked exceptions (unreported exception…), wrong generics (List<String> vs List<Integer>)
  • Encapsulation & access modifiers — private/protected/public, packages: teams can hide implementation and expose only APIs → hundreds of engineers work on one codebase without stepping on each other
  • Interfaces & polymorphism — code against PaymentGateway, swap implementations (Card, UPI, Wallet) — the basis of testing (mocks) and plug-in architecture
  • Package naming (com.company.module) — unique global names, layered structure
  • Refactoring-friendly — IDEs (IntelliJ IDEA, Eclipse, VS Code) understand Java deeply: rename a method across 5,000 files safely
  • Javadoc & annotations — self-documenting APIs (Section 21)
Language featureBig-team benefit
Static typing + genericsfewer runtime type bugs; safe refactors
Checked exceptionserror paths are visible in method signatures
Interfaces / abstract classesclear contracts between teams
Packages + access modifiersmodular ownership
Annotations + reflectionframeworks (Spring, JPA) configure behaviour declaratively
Reason 6 — Robustness & Security

Course link: Section 4.6 (features: robust, secure), Section 18 (exceptions).

  • No pointer arithmetic, bounds-checked arrays (ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException) → the classic buffer-overflow and use-after-free bug classes of C/C++ largely don't exist
  • Byte-code verifier at class-loading time (Section 4.5) rejects malformed/malicious byte code
  • Exception handling + try-with-resources → predictable failure handling, no leaked connections/files
  • Mature security libraries — JCA/JCE (crypto), JSSE (TLS), authentication/authorisation frameworks (Spring Security), regular security patches through LTS updates
  • Static analysis & dependency scanning are well developed for Java (Maven/Gradle ecosystems)
Security is never "automatic": companies still patch libraries (e.g. logging/serialization vulnerabilities in third-party code). The *language* removes many memory-safety risks; *process* handles the rest.
Reason 7 — A Huge, Battle-Tested Ecosystem
NeedJava-world standardNote
Web/REST microservicesSpring Boot, Jakarta EE, Quarkus, Micronaut, HelidonSpring Boot is the de-facto standard for microservices; Netflix is famous for using it
Database accessJDBC (Section 28), JPA/Hibernate, jOOQone API for every relational database — drivers per vendor
Messaging & streamingApache Kafka (born at LinkedIn; JVM-based), ActiveMQ, RabbitMQ clientsevent-driven architectures
SearchElasticsearch / Lucene (Java)search & analytics
Big dataHadoop, Spark, Flink, Cassandra, Beam (JVM)data pipelines
Build & dependenciesMaven, Gradlereproducible builds
TestingJUnit, Mockito, Testcontainerstest culture built in
App serversTomcat, Jetty, Undertow, WebLogic, WebSpheremature deployment options
ObservabilityMicrometer, OpenTelemetry, JFR, JMXmetrics/tracing out of the box
Desktop/GUIAWT, Swing, JavaFX (Sections 29–31)internal tools, trading UIs
  • Why the ecosystem mattersa company doesn't need to *build* queues, ORMs, HTTP servers, schedulers, metrics — they exist, are open source, and have years of production hardening
  • Stack Overflow answers, books, courses, conference talks: whatever problem you have, someone has solved it in Java
Reason 8 — Talent Pool & Hiring
  • Millions of Java developers, taught in universities and training programmes worldwide; IT service companies (TCS, Infosys, Wipro, Accenture…) run large Java practices
  • Easy to staff, replace and scale a team — a critical concern when a system must live 10–20 years and engineers change jobs
  • Onboardingnew hires understand a Java codebase quickly because of conventions (packages, layers, naming, Javadoc)
  • Career depthfrom beginner to architect — the same language across backend, big data, Android (existing apps), and tooling
  • India-specific noteJava is a staple of campus recruitment, banking/insurance IT and service-company projects
Reason 9 — Governance, Licensing Choice & Long-Term Support
  • OpenJDK is open source (GPLv2 with Classpath Exception); many vendors ship certified builds → no single-vendor lock-in
  • LTS releases with years of security patches (Java 25: Oracle updates to 2030, paid extended support to 2033; other vendors such as Corretto/Temurin/Azul up to 2031–2033) → predictable upgrade budgets
  • Standards process (JCP, JEPs) → changes are discussed publicly and compatibility is protected
  • Licence awarenessOracle changed licensing terms in 2019 and again for later JDK versions; a large share of Java professionals report concern about Oracle pricing (survey figure ~92 %) — which is exactly why many enterprises standardise on OpenJDK builds (Temurin, Corretto, Azul, etc.) — a choice Java's openness allows
Always check the licence of the *specific vendor build and version* you deploy — Oracle's terms differ per release (e.g. permissive licensing for JDK 21 updates ends with the October 2026 CPU; Oracle JDK 25 is free-use).
Reason 10 — A Language That Keeps Modernising Without Breaking

Java 8 → 25 added functional and data-oriented features while old code still runs.

FeatureSinceBenefit in big codebases
Lambdas, method references, streams (Section 22)8shorter, declarative collection code
var, text blocks10 / 15less boilerplate
record classes16one-line immutable data carriers (DTOs)
Sealed classes, pattern matching17+safer domain models
Virtual threads21massively concurrent servers without reactive complexity
Generational ZGC21low-pause memory management
GraalVM native image (ecosystem)–fast start-up, lower memory for serverless/containers
Modern Java still reads like the course you learnedrecord Order(int id, String customer, double amount) { }         // 1 line replaces constructor+getters+equals+toString

List<Order> orders = List.of(new Order(1, "Ravi", 500), new Order(2, "Anu", 1200));
double big = orders.stream().filter(o -> o.amount() > 1000).mapToDouble(Order::amount).sum();   // lambdas + method reference

try (var executor = java.util.concurrent.Executors.newVirtualThreadPerTaskExecutor()) {          // Java 21 virtual threads
    orders.forEach(o -> executor.submit(() -> process(o)));
}

Case Study — Netflix (the Best-Documented Example)
1

Java + Spring Boot at the core

thousands of services; frontends (TV, phone, browser) talk to a federated GraphQL API gateway; backend teams evolve services independently

2

Stuck on JDK 8 for years

dependencies on a custom in-house framework and patched libraries delayed upgrades — *shows how sticky large Java systems are*

3

Company-wide migration

to Spring Boot and newer JDKs with automated code transformation — Java's compatibility made a mechanical, tool-driven upgrade possible

4

Java 17 → 21 (easier than 8 → 17)

adopted virtual threads (thread-per-request style at scale) and generational ZGC (lower pauses, higher utilisation)

5

Real-world lesson

intermittent hangs traced to virtual threads pinned on locks + embedded Tomcat; the team reproduced, fixed and published it

  • Takeaway for youJava at scale means upgrade discipline, observability and careful adoption — not just syntax. The topics you learned (threads, synchronization, exceptions, collections, JVM memory) are *exactly* what these engineers debug.
  • Also useful vocabulary*microservices*, *API gateway*, *GraphQL*, *dependency injection*, *fault tolerance*, *canary rollout*

A tiny taste (beyond the course): what the layers look like in Spring Boot@RestController                                    // annotation → read via reflection at start-up
@RequestMapping("/orders")
class OrderController {
    private final OrderService service;            // interface/class injected by the framework (no `new`)
    OrderController(OrderService service) { this.service = service; }

    @GetMapping("/{id}")
    Order get(@PathVariable int id) {              // exception → HTTP 404 via a global handler
        return service.find(id).orElseThrow(() -> new OrderNotFoundException(id));   // lambda + custom exception
    }
}
interface OrderRepository extends JpaRepository<Order, Integer> { }      // generics + interface; implementation generated
This is why the course order matters: OOP + interfaces + exceptions + collections + threads + JDBC are the muscles; Spring Boot is just the framework that wires them together.

I5

Honest Counter-Points, Trade-offs & How to Answer "Why Java?"

Lectures balanced view
Where Java Is *Not* the Best Choice
AreaBetter-fit language(s)Why
AI / ML / data sciencePython (libraries, notebooks)ecosystem and prototyping speed; Java has DJL, Deeplearning4j, Tribuo, but Python dominates
Front-end webJavaScript / TypeScriptruns in the browser
New Android appsKotlin (Google's preferred language)more concise; interoperates with Java
Systems / OS / embedded / game enginesC, C++, Rustno GC pauses, direct hardware control
Small CLI tools & cloud plumbingGotiny static binaries, fast start-up
Quick scriptsPython, Bashno compile step
  • Verbositymore boilerplate than Kotlin/Python — reduced by record, var, lambdas, and Lombok-style tools
  • Start-up time & memorya JVM warms up; mitigations: GraalVM native image, Class-Data Sharing, smaller frameworks (Quarkus, Micronaut), and virtual threads instead of heavy reactive stacks
  • Licensing confusionOracle's changing terms worry many teams → use OpenJDK builds (Temurin, Corretto, Azul…)
  • Slow upgrades31 % still on Java 8 — legacy drag; security patches and features are missed
  • Popularity trendTIOBE share is at a multi-decade low (7.54 %) even though the installed base is huge — *market share of new projects* is more contested than *installed base*
  • New features have pitfallse.g. Netflix's virtual-thread pinning incident; adopt with testing
How to Answer "Why Is Java Popular in Big Product Companies?" (Interview Script)
1

Start with the one-line answer

"Because it combines portability, performance, safety and a huge ecosystem with long-term stability — ideal for systems that must scale and live for a decade."

2

Give 3 technical reasons (pick from the course)

① JVM + bytecode → run anywhere; JIT + modern GC → high throughput ② strong typing + OOP + packages → large teams stay consistent ③ built-in concurrency (threads → virtual threads) + mature libraries

3

Give 2 business reasons

① backward compatibility protects existing investment (upgrade in place — e.g. Netflix moved 8 → 17 → 21) ② huge talent pool + multi-vendor OpenJDK + predictable LTS releases → low risk

4

Name the ecosystem

Spring Boot, Hibernate/JPA, Kafka, Maven/Gradle, JUnit, Kubernetes-friendly

5

Add one honest limitation

"Python leads AI, Kotlin is preferred for new Android apps, and startup/memory costs exist — but the JVM is improving (virtual threads, ZGC, native image)."

6

Finish with your own link

"I learned the fundamentals — OOP, collections, exceptions, threads, JDBC — that these systems are built on."

Numbers to remember (Sept 2026): TIOBE #4, Java 25 = current LTS, Java 27 released 15 Sep 2026, next LTS Java 29 (Sep 2027), Java 21/17/8 all common in production.
Quick Interview Q&A — Java in Industry
QuestionShort answer
Why do banks prefer Java?correctness, mature transaction/DB support (JDBC/JPA), security, backward compatibility, huge talent pool, long-term support
Is Java dead?No — #4 on TIOBE and among the most-used languages; enormous installed base and steady LTS releases; but growth is slower than Python
Why does Java not run out of memory like C?automatic garbage collection and bounds-checked arrays — no manual free
What made Java fit microservices?Spring Boot, embedded servers, container-friendly JARs, fast-improving start-up (native image), virtual threads
What is an LTS?Long-Term Support release with multi-year patches (8, 11, 17, 21, 25)
Which JDK should a new project use?the current LTS (Java 25 in Sept 2026), or 21 if a dependency requires it
Java vs Kotlin?both run on the JVM; Kotlin is more concise and preferred for new Android apps; Java dominates server-side enterprise
What are virtual threads?JVM-managed lightweight threads (Java 21) that make thread-per-request servers scale
Is Java free?OpenJDK is free (GPL + Classpath Exception); check each vendor's licence — Oracle's terms vary by version
One-line reason companies stay on Java?rewriting is riskier and costlier than evolving a stable platform that keeps modernising
